# CA review pack — Mr. Chartist Invoice

Prepared 26 Oct 2026 for the app's reviewing Chartered Accountant. Baseline: branch `claude/elegant-cannon-cwapiv` at
`3765b16` (Phases 0–8, Rounds 2–5 merged).

## What this is and how to answer

Mr. Chartist Invoice is an offline invoicing and accounting app for Indian businesses. It prepares GST, TDS / TCS,
payroll, accounting and export **working papers**. Every screen carries the line "working paper — verify with your CA".
The app files nothing and connects to no portal.

The developers built each statutory rule from notifications, circulars, portal advisories and secondary sources as they
understood them. This pack lists **every point they asked a CA to confirm**, from the 66 feature documents and the
Round 2–5 specifications. Repeated points have been merged.

Each item gives:
- **Question** — what we need you to confirm.
- **App's assumption** — what the app does today.
- **Where** — the file under `src/lib/` (unless another path is given) and the rule-table key. Dated rule tables hold
  one row per change. A document uses the row in force on its own date, and old rows are never edited, so a
  correction is a **new dated row**.
- **From** — the date from which the app applies the value (the latest row unless stated). "—" means the point is
  not date-keyed.
- **Source used** — the citation stored in the code or the feature document. "(unverified)" means the developers
  flagged the value themselves.

**How to reply.** Quote the item id (for example `G7`) and choose one:
- **OK** — the assumption is right.
- **Change** — give the correct value, the date it applies from, and the source.
- **Depends** — say on what.

Items marked ★ are the ones beta testers' figures are most likely to depend on. Please answer these first.

## 1. GST

### 1.1 Rates, thresholds and values

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 ★ | Is the B2CL threshold ₹1 lakh for invoices from 1 Aug 2024? | Unregistered inter-State invoices above ₹1 lakh go to B2CL, and to B2CS otherwise. Invoices before that date use ₹2.5 lakh. | `tax-rules.ts` `B2CL_THRESHOLD_RULES` | 2024-08-01 | Notification 12/2024-CT |
| G2 ★ | From 22 Sep 2025, is the rate list 0, 0.1, 0.25, 1, 1.5, 3, 5, 6, 18, 28 and 40% (12% removed; 28% kept only for tobacco / pan masala until compensation cess ends)? | Rate pickers and the exceptions report ("rate outside slab") use the list in force on the document date. An old 12% bill being edited keeps its rate. | `tax-rules.ts` `GST_SLAB_RULES`; `gst-guards.ts` `gstExceptions` | 2025-09-22 | 56th GST Council; Notification 9/2025-CT(R) as understood (unverified) |
| G3 | No GST on advances for goods from 15 Nov 2017, and GST on advances for services on receipt (Sec 13(2)). Is that right? | A goods advance is reported in neither 11A nor 3B. A service advance is taxed in the month received and adjusted in 11B. | `tax-rules.ts` `ADVANCE_GST_ON_GOODS_RULES`, `ADVANCE_GST_ON_SERVICES_RULES` | 2017-11-15 | Notification 66/2017-CT; CGST Act Sec 13(2) |
| G4 | When an advance is refunded (Rule 51), should its tax be reported in **11B of the refund month** or as an **amendment of 11A** of the receipt month? | 11B of the refund month. | `advances.ts`; `gst-reports.ts` (tables `at` / `txpd`) | — | Developers' reading |
| G5 | Taxable freight / other charges: is the rate of the **highest-rated line** correct for a mixed-rate invoice (Sec 15)? | Taxable charges take the highest line rate and are reported as one more rate row under the main HSN. | `invoice-calc.ts`; `docs/features/freight-fx.md` | — | Sec 15 as understood |
| G6 | Interest or late fee charged to a customer for delayed payment: is it part of the value of the supply at the principal supply's rate (Sec 15(2)(d))? | The late-fee draft invoice takes the rate, HSN / SAC and reverse-charge setting of the largest line of the original. | `late-fee.ts` (`principalTaxRate`, `principalItem`) | — | Sec 15(2)(d) CGST Act |
| G7 | Pure-agent reimbursements (Rule 33): may they stay outside the taxable value when the user ticks "pure agent"? | The line is billed at 0% with a note. Whether the Rule 33 conditions are met is left to the user. | `timesheets.ts`; `annexure.ts` | — | Rule 33 CGST Rules |
| G8 | Settlement discount / kasar after the supply: is a **financial credit note without GST** right (Circular 92/11/2019)? May the GST variant be used only if the discount was agreed before supply and the buyer reverses ITC (Sec 15(3)(b))? | The default is a financial credit note `FCN/FY…` with no GST, kept out of GSTR-1 and the composition returns. The optional pro-rata GST variant is offered on the dialog with no proof check. | `financial-note.ts` (`isNonGstFinancialNote`); `settlement.ts` (`gst_variant`) | — | Circular 92/11/2019-GST as understood |
| G9 | Bad debts: is it right that **no output GST is reversed** when a bill is written off? | The write-off posts Dr Bad debts / Cr Debtors only. GST already paid stays paid. | `write-off.ts` | — | No GST relief on bad debts (developers' reading) |
| G10 | Buy-X-get-Y and bill-value offers: is GST due only on the price charged, with the free item as a ₹0 line and no ITC reversal? | The free goods are a ₹0 line (left out of the HSN summary). Bill-value offers are a discount before tax. | `offers.ts`, `pricing.ts` | — | Circular 92/11/2019-GST |
| G11 | Loyalty-point redemption: is it a discount at the time of supply (Sec 15(3)(a)), with GST on the reduced value? | Yes: it is added to the bill's amount discount. | `loyalty.ts` (`applyRedemption`) | — | Sec 15(3)(a) |
| G12 ★ | Counter (POS) sales to a customer from another State: is the place of supply our own State when goods are handed over at the counter? | Intra-State by default. A "deliver to the customer's State" tick makes it IGST. | `pos.ts` (`makeDraft`) | — | Sec 10(1)(a)/(c) IGST Act |

Which notes are mandatory for small companies? Should the pack carry a "prepared by" block and the audit-trail chain head? | Automatic + text notes. Entity wording per type. | `ledger/annual-pack.ts`, `ledger/notes-auto.ts` | — | Schedule III Div I |

| AC4 | RCM / import GST stays on 2310 / 2311 until a payment or journal clears it; customs duty is not modelled in the ledger. | As stated. | `ledger/postings*.ts` | — | — |

| AC6 | Exchange difference: realised on receipt to ledger 4930. Period-end revaluation of receivables (AS 11) is a **report only**, not posted. Forex bank revaluation is posted and not reversed. Is that presentation right? | As stated. GST keeps the invoice-date rate (Rule 34). | `fx-gain.ts`; `forex-bank.ts` | — | AS 11 / Ind AS 21 |
| AC7 | Settlement discount: book it as an indirect expense ("Discount allowed") or as a reduction of revenue? | Indirect expense in the P&L and in Sales-by-customer. | `financial-note.ts`; `stats.ts`; `sales-reports.ts` | — | Product decision pending |
| AC8 | Inter-branch supplies between two of our GSTINs post to 4030 / 5020 "Branch transfer" so they net to nil on consolidation. Is that acceptable? | As stated. | `branch-transfer.ts` | — | — |
| AC9 | Order costing (production): a management working (materials standard + a typed conversion rate). Variances are **not posted**. The yield variance is carved out of usage on closed orders. | As stated. | `production/costing.ts` | — | — |
| AC10 | Landed cost: the freight / CHA bill stays an expense in Books while the landed-cost voucher restates stock valuation. Should freight inward sit in Purchases for gross profit? | As stated. | `landed-voucher.ts`, `landed-cost.ts` | — | — |
| AC11 | Loyalty points: an outstanding-points × point-value report only. Do Ind AS 115 / AS 9 entities need a provision journal? | No journal. | `retail/loyalty-tiers.ts`; `loyalty.ts` | — | Ind AS 115 |
| AC12 | Opening balances: the residual goes to "1090 Opening balance difference" (never forced to zero). Income / expense openings dated before 1 April roll into reserves. | As stated. | `ledger/openings.ts`, `ledger/postings-openings.ts` | — | Tally practice |

Is CSV + an integrity text acceptable evidence, or is a signed PDF expected? Should the pack include the backup log for Rule 3(5)? The app's backups are user-located. | Auditor pack = CSV + integrity text. | `audit-edits.ts`, `audit-chain.ts`; `trust/auditor-pack.ts` | — | Rule 11(g); Rule 3(5) |
| AC14 | Recycle bin keeps deleted rows for 30 days (5 MB cap). Is that consistent with the DPDP Act 2023 (the owner can delete permanently)? Should rows replaced by a restore or a sync "Use theirs" also go to the bin? | 30 days. Not captured on restore / sync. | `trust/recycle-bin.ts` | — | DPDP Act 2023 |

Is exactly ₹7,500 in the 5% band? Is a row missing for the July 2018 change from declared tariff to transaction value (N 13/2018-CT(R) as understood)? | ₹7,500.00 → 5%; ₹7,500.01 → 18%. Luxury tax is not computed: it was subsumed into GST from 1 Jul 2017. If a State or local body still levies a tourism
or similar cess on rooms, the app does not know it. Please name any that apply.

**R7-C build notes (what the GST desk, Form C desk and reports decided — please confirm with the item):**
- HT1 / HT2 / HT14 — a room line's unit-day value is its taxable value ÷ quantity (unit-nights), tested on the invoice
  date (D6). An extra-bed / extra-person line on the same invoice may carry a passing room line's higher rate (one
  unit-day value, D2); any other mismatch is a "wrong band" check. Composition and Bill-of-Supply documents are left out (HT16).
- HT3 — the previous-FY test reads the stays' night snapshots (room + extra bed / person + plan meals; meals priced
  separately and complimentary / house-use rooms left out) or a figure typed for a year before the app; invoices are
  not read. An opt-in / opt-out must be filed 1 Jan – 31 Mar of the calendar year the FY starts in; Annexure VIII within
  15 days of the ARN date typed with it. The latest valid declaration up to the FY decides and continues until changed;
  status is per premises (`property_id`; the desk shows the main premises).
- HT4 — the exempt-like turnover added to E in the Rule 42 / 43 working is the taxable value of rooms at a no-ITC band
  **and** F&B at the non-specified 5%-no-ITC rate (credit notes net off). Rows before 22 Sep 2025 carry ITC, so
  nothing is added. The ITC check fires once per FY when every invoiced room-night is in a no-ITC band and any bill
  claims ITC (not RCM, net of any reversal) — a warning only.
- HT5 — any IGST hotel invoice is flagged.
- HT13 — the Form C deadline is the check-in instant + the rule's hours (on the local check-in day), so it runs across
  midnight; the bell warns from 6 hours before and turns red when overdue; the penalty is shown as "up to ₹50,000 per
  case (as reported) — verify". The data sheet follows `FORM_C_FIELDS` (portal order as understood).
- HT17 — reports count complimentary / house-use rooms as occupied (occupancy %) but not in ADR; a day without a night
  audit is computed from the stays and posted folio lines.

**QA9 notes (hotel fixes, `docs/prd/qa-round-9.md` — please confirm with the item; no id added):**
- HT1 / HT11 — **tax-inclusive plans.** The guest pays the agreed inclusive price. The booking snapshot solves each night
  with the rule in force on the night; the check-out invoice taxes at the rule on the **invoice date** and re-solves the
  value from the inclusive price (value + GST = price). (a) **Band gap:** from 22 Sep 2025 an inclusive price of
  ₹7,875.01–₹8,850 fits no band (÷ 1.05 is above ₹7,500; ÷ 1.18 is below it). The app takes the higher band: 18% on a
  value below ₹7,500 (e.g. ₹8,000 → ₹6,779.66 + 18%). Is that right, or should such a price be treated as ₹7,500 + 5%?
  (b) Nights before 22 Sep 2025 invoiced after it are re-solved at 5% (₹7,000 → ₹6,666.67), so the hotel's net rises.
- HT2 — an allowance not linked to a night is spread over the stay's nights pro rata before the band is found (D2:
  value after discount).
- HT3 — the previous-FY value now reads the folio lines actually posted per room-night (after linked allowances; an
  early check-out counts only the nights stayed; meals priced separately are F&B and left out), and each premises
  (branch profile) is judged on its own stays.
- HT10 — a cancellation of a booking whose company paid the advance is invoiced to the company (its GSTIN) and that
  advance is adjusted; only the rest is refunded.
- HT11 (a) — the 31 March interim invoice is taken on 31 March before the night audit and includes the 31 March night
  (posted early), dated 31 March in the old FY.

Is an agent acting only for agriculturists free of compulsory registration (Circular 57/31/2018 + corrigendum)? Which produce loses "agricultural produce" status (dal, rice, jaggery, cotton ginned)? | Aadat exempt for agricultural produce, 18% SAC 996111 otherwise; the user picks the produce class per item. No registration advice is given. | `trade/tr-patti.ts`; charge head `own_rate` | — | N 12/2017-CT(R) entry 54; Circular 57/31/2018-GST + corrigendum (state copies; secondary for entry 54(g)) |
| TR10 ★ | **Kachi aadat** (agent sells the principal's goods and invoices the buyer **in its own name**): the agent is a Schedule I para 3 agent and the sale is the agent's supply for GST (GSTR-1 in the agent's return), while in the books the gross sale is transferred to the principal by the patti and only the commission is income. Is that presentation right? Does the principal → agent transfer need a document (delivery challan / invoice) when the principal is registered? | As stated; arrivals record the inward goods; no principal-side document is generated by us. | `trade/tr-arrivals.ts`, `trade/tr-patti.ts` | — | Schedule I para 3; Circular 57/31/2018-GST |
| TR11 | Weight deductions (tare per bag, dhalta per quintal or %): the invoice value is on the **net** weight after deductions. Is any deduction (e.g. dharmada, kat) a discount that must be shown separately under Sec 15(3)? | Net quantity × rate; deductions shown as text on the line. | `trade/tr-weight.ts` | — | Trade practice (developers' reading) |
| TR12 | Brokerage paid to an **unregistered** broker: no GST under reverse charge (Sec 9(4) applies only to notified classes) — right? Brokerage paid by the other party (memo only) creates nothing in our books — right? | No RCM; memo rows not posted. | `trade/tr-brokers.ts` | — | Sec 9(4) as amended from 1 Feb 2019 (developers' reading) |

Are the 27EQ codes 6CA–6CJ and the 2025-Act payment codes 1068–1074 right? | 2% for all from 1 Apr 2026, timber flagged unverified; Form 127 named after 1 Apr 2026. | `trading-rules.ts` `TCS_SPECIFIED_GOODS_RULES`; `tds-rules.ts` `TCS_27EQ_CODES` | 2026-04-01 | Finance Act 2026 as reported (taxguru); tdsman (secondary) |

Under the 2025 Act is it Sec 394(1) Table Sl. 6 or 6.D(a) / (b)? Which 27EQ / Form 143 code applies to luxury goods? | Sl. 6 label "(verify with your CA)"; motor vehicle code 6CL; luxury goods code left blank with a warning. | `trading-rules.ts` `TCS_1F_RULES`; `ita2025.ts` | 2016-06-01; 2025-04-22 | N 36/2025 (taxmann, taxguru — secondary) |

## 9. Retail shops (Round 9, ids RT1–RT22)

Added with `docs/prd/round-9.md`, which covers kirana / supermarket, pharmacy, apparel / footwear / textiles, electronics
and mobiles, and jewellery.

- These are **planned** behaviours. The files named under "Where" are the ones Round 9 will create
  (`src/lib/retail-rules.ts`, `src/lib/retail/rt-*.ts`).
- Ids use the prefix `RT` so they do not clash with other rounds' ids.
- Some items are also questions for someone other than a CA: RT7 for the drug inspector, RT15 for a telecom lawyer and
  RT19 for the BIS. Please say so where that applies.
- Raw sources: the round-9 research notes (P = primary text read, S = secondary).

### 9.1 Every shop, kirana and apparel

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RT1 ★ | **Apparel / footwear GST by sale value.** From 22 Sep 2025: garments / made-ups ≤ ₹2,500 per piece 5%, above 18%; footwear ≤ ₹2,500 per pair 5%, above 18%. Earlier rows: apparel ≤ ₹1,000 5% / else 12% (from 1 Jul 2017); footwear ≤ ₹500 5% / else 18% (2017), ≤ ₹1,000 from 27 Jul 2018. Is exactly ₹2,500 in the 5% band? Are the earlier rows and dates right? Do made-ups (Chapter 63) follow the apparel rule? | ≤ ₹2,500.00 → 5%; ₹2,500.01 → 18%. Rows as listed. The 2018 footwear row and made-ups are marked unverified. | `retail-rules.ts` `PRICE_BAND_RULES`; `retail/rt-bands.ts` | 2025-09-22 | N 9/2025-CT(R) as reported (accountune, Bata notice, CLE — secondary); earlier rows from memory (unverified) |
| RT2 | **Which value decides the band?** (a) Per-piece taxable value after the line discount and the line's share of an invoice-level amount discount (offer, loyalty), excluding GST. (b) For MRP-inclusive pricing, test the 5% band first. (c) Bundles ("3 for ₹1,999"): line value ÷ pieces. (d) A later credit note (post-sale discount) does not re-rate the original line. Is each of these right? | As stated. | `retail/rt-bands.ts` `bandRate` | — | Sec 15 (transaction value per piece) — developers' reading |
| RT3 | **Alterations.** A free alteration with a garment sale is part of the sale (no separate supply). Is a charged alteration a separate tailoring service, SAC 998821 at 5% or at 18%? Tailor's charges from an unregistered tailor: no GST and no RCM? | Free → nothing. Charged → SAC 998821 at 5% (unverified). Unregistered tailor → no GST / RCM. | `retail-rules.ts` `ALTERATION_RULES`; `retail/rt-alterations.ts` | 2017-07-01 | Developers' reading |
| RT4 ★ | **MRP.** (a) Sale above MRP: the app blocks (or warns on) a sale priced above the MRP inclusive of GST, per base unit, after discounts (Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules 2011, Rule 18(2)). (b) GST cut: stock printed with the old MRP is priced at MRP × (100 + new) / (100 + old) when the user marks the batch; never raised on an increase. Is (b) right for GST purposes, and acceptable under the Legal Metrology / NPPA advisories of Sep 2025? Is any anti-profiteering style record still expected? | As stated. The effective MRP is a pricing aid, not a statutory computation. | `retail/rt-mrp.ts` (`mrpGuard`, `mrpAfterRateChange`) | 2025-09-22 (rate change) | LM Rules 2011 r. 18(2) (casemine, Lok Sabha reply); EY alert on the 22 Sep 2025 cuts (secondary) |
| RT5 | **Free goods received** on a supplier's bill ("10 + 1"): GST and ITC only on the value charged, no ITC reversal for the free units, and cost spread over paid + free units for stock valuation. Right? | As stated. | `purchase-normalize.ts` (`freeQty`) | — | Circular 92/11/2019-GST (read from the supplier's side) |
| RT6 | **Home delivery.** (a) A delivery charge on a goods bill: part of the value at the goods' rate (as G5), or a separate supply of transport at its own rate? (b) Cash collected by our own rider is booked on the delivery day in a "Cash with riders" cash-in-hand ledger, and moved to counter cash on settlement. Is that acceptable? | Charge in value at the highest line rate (G5). Receipts as stated. | `retail/rt-delivery.ts` | — | Sec 15(2)(c); G5 |

### 9.2 Pharmacy

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RT7 ★ | **Drugs & Cosmetics Rules, Rule 65.** Registers kept from our own sales: Schedule H1 (prescriber name and address, patient, drug, batch, quantity), kept 3 years; Schedule X, with "prescription retained" and the patient's address (prescription kept 2 years per secondary sources); optional Schedule H; a narcotic / psychotropic stock register (opening, received, sold, closing per batch). Cash memo copies kept 3 years. Is the retention right? Does the X register need a fixed format? Must the registered pharmacist's name be on the bill? (A drug-inspector question as much as a CA one.) | Registers printed as books + CSV. Deletion inside the retention period is warned, not blocked. Pharmacist printed when set. | `retail-rules.ts` `PHARMA_RETENTION_RULES`; `retail/rt-pharmacy-registers.ts` | 1945 Rules (current text) | okcredit guide, Kohima district order (secondary). Draft amendment of Sep 2026 (CCTV) not applied |

Is the check "MRP per unit ≤ ceiling × (1 + GST%)" right? Does the 10%-a-year cap on non-scheduled MRP increases concern a retailer at all? | Warning only, from ceilings the user types or imports per item, dated. | `retail/rt-pharmacy-prices.ts` | — | DPCO 2013 (developers' reading, not re-read) |
| RT9 | **Medicines' GST and doctor referrals.** (a) From 22 Sep 2025 most medicines are 5% (33 life-saving drugs and 3 others nil). The app ships no schedule: the user enters rates per HSN in the HSN master. Is that acceptable for a working paper? (b) A chemist's payments or gifts to doctors for referrals: the app has **no** commission field and books nothing. Please confirm that no special disallowance / TDS treatment is expected from the app (Sec 37 Explanation 1; Apex Laboratories, SC 2022). | As stated. | `hsn-master.ts` (existing); `retail/rt-pharmacy-rx.ts` | 2025-09-22 | EY alert, Lakshmikumaran (secondary) |

| RT10 ★ | **EMI / consumer finance.** (a) The invoice is to the customer for the full price. (b) The financed part is moved to the financier (debtor) by journal. (c) The financier pays net of subvention / processing fee, which the app books as the financier's own taxable supply to us (SAC 997119, 18%, ITC per its invoice), not as a discount on our sale. (d) A retailer-funded "no-cost EMI" discount shown on the invoice at sale reduces the value (Sec 15(3)(a)). (e) A brand's cashback paid later does not. (b) The old device is bought from an individual on our own purchase voucher (`URD/FY…`), with no GST, no ITC and no RCM (Sec 9(4) limited to notified classes since 1 Feb 2019). Is GST on the full value right, rather than on the net cash? Is our self-made voucher adequate evidence? | Full value. Voucher as stated. The exchange settles the sale as a no-cash receipt. | `retail/rt-buyback.ts`; `no-cash.ts` (`exchange_purchase_id`) | — | Sec 7, 15; Rule 27 (barter) — developers' reading |

Taxable value = sale − purchase price of that serial (nil when negative), GST inclusive. The purchase-value part is reported as non-taxable. Does refurbishment (screen / battery change) keep it within Rule 32(5)? How should the non-taxable part appear in GSTR-1 (nil / exempt / not reported)? May the margin scheme be used on a B2B sale? | As stated. Nil / exempt row. B2B with ITC demanded → refused with a warning. | `retail/rt-buyback.ts` | 2017-07-01 | Rule 32(5) CGST Rules; AAR Karnataka Aadhya Gold 2018 (recalled) |

(b) Replacement parts / units given free under the manufacturer's warranty: no GST and no ITC reversal (Circular 195/07/2023-GST as understood)? (c) Does (b) hold when the retailer, not the brand, replaces under its own extended warranty? | Service line at 18% (unverified SAC) + parts lines. Warranty replacement: no sale, stock swap only. | `retail-rules.ts` `WARRANTY_REPLACEMENT_RULES`; `retail/rt-service-jobs.ts` | 2023-07-17 | Circular 195/07/2023-GST (recalled, not re-read) |
| RT14 | **Cash paid for used goods.** Cash paid to one person in a day above ₹10,000 for old gold or a used device is disallowed under Sec 40A(3) (from 1 Apr 2017), and the app warns. Which section is it under the Income-tax Act 2025? Do any Rule 6DD exceptions fit a shop buying from walk-in customers? | Warning above ₹10,000 per person per day. | `retail-rules.ts` `CASH_PAYMENT_LIMIT_RULES`; `retail/rt-cash-guard.ts` | 2017-04-01 | Sec 40A(3) (recalled); 2025-Act section number not confirmed |

The app keeps a check log and warns on a resale without a "clear" check in the last 30 days. It never calls the portal. Is a 30-day window sensible? Is any per-check fee or record-keeping period prescribed? (A telecom-law question.) | As stated. | `retail-rules.ts` `IMEI_CHECK_RULES`; `retail/rt-imei.ts` | 2025-10-22 | conventuslaw, scconline (secondary) |

(a) On an exchange, is GST due on the **full** value of the new jewellery (the app's default) or only on the net amount paid (as some sources say)? (b) Is old gold that is melted and reused eligible for the margin scheme? (AAR Aadhya Gold says no.) | Full value. No margin scheme for melted gold. | RT18 ★ | **Cash and identity.** (a) Sec 269ST: no cash receipt of ₹2 lakh or more from a person in a day / per transaction / per event. (b) PAN, or the Form 60 / Form 97 declaration, for a sale above ₹2 lakh in any mode (Rule 114B). (c) 206C(1D) TCS on cash jewellery sales is not applied after 31 Mar 2017. (d) PMLA: cash transactions of ₹10 lakh or more by a dealer in precious metals / stones need KYC and records (notification of 28 Dec 2020 as recalled). What are the Income-tax Act 2025 / Rules 2026 references for (a) and (b)? | (a) Block for jewellery profiles, warn for electronics. (b) Prompt, required for jewellery. (c) None. (d) A note only. | `retail-rules.ts` `CASH_RECEIPT_LIMIT_RULES`, `PAN_SALE_LIMIT_RULES`, `PMLA_CASH_RULES`; `retail/rt-cash-guard.ts`; `ita2025.ts` labels | 2017-04-01; 2016-01-01; 2020-12-28 | Sec 269ST / 271DA, Rule 114B (iifl, taxmann — secondary); PMLA notification date unverified |
| RT19 | **Hallmarking (BIS).** HUID (6 characters) has been mandatory for hallmarked gold since 1 Apr 2023 in notified districts; silver is voluntary from 1 Sep 2025. Is the hallmarking charge (₹45 + GST for gold, ₹35 for silver per piece, as reported) a separate taxable line, or part of the 3% jewellery value as assumed? (A BIS question as much as a CA one.) | As stated. The hallmarking charge is inside the 3% line value and printed separately. | `retail-rules.ts` `HALLMARK_RULES`; `retail/rt-jewel-line.ts` | 2023-04-01; 2025-09-01 | BIS FAQ (bis.gov.in, read in part); lakshmisri; gjepc / indianjeweller (secondary) |
| RT20 | **Karigar (job worker).** Metal issued to a karigar is tracked by fine weight; finished goods received consume it plus an agreed wastage allowance. (a) Must a registered jeweller file ITC-04 and issue job-work challans (Rule 55) for every issue to a karigar? (b) Is the wastage allowance a normal loss with no ITC reversal (Sec 17(5)(h) not attracted)? | Fine-weight ledger. ITC-04 / challan only when the user opts in to the job-work model. Does it cover silver articles and imitation jewellery (7117)? | As stated. 7117 is not treated as exempt. | `retail-rules.ts` `EWAY_EXEMPT_CHAPTER_RULES` | 2017-07-01 | Rule 138(14) annexure (recalled) |

## 10. Service businesses (Round 10, ids SV1–SV30, SV41–SV48)

Added with `docs/prd/round-10.md`, which covers clinics, dentists and diagnostic labs; salons, spas and beauty parlours;
gyms, fitness studios and yoga; coaching and training institutes; and vehicle workshops and AC / appliance service
centres.

- These are **planned** behaviours. The files named under "Where" are the ones Round 10 will create
  (`src/lib/service-rules.ts`, `src/lib/services/sv-*.ts`).
- Ids use the prefix `SV` so they do not clash with other rounds' ids.
- Some items are also questions for someone other than a CA: SV7 for the state clinical-establishments authority or the
  medical council, SV18 and SV19 for a lawyer, and the depreciation table in SV22 for the motor insurer. Please say so
  where that applies.
- Raw sources: the round-10 research notes (P = primary text read, S = secondary).

### 10.1 Clinics, dentists and diagnostic labs

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SV1 | **Health care exemption scope.** Entry 74 of N 12/2017-CT(R) exempts health care services by a clinical establishment, an authorised medical practitioner or para-medics. Does it cover dentists, physiotherapists, AYUSH practitioners, standalone diagnostic labs and imaging centres, and lab-to-lab (outsourced) testing billed to another lab? Is sample-collection commission paid to a collection centre a taxable supply to us (18%)? | All of them exempt as health care; lab-to-lab testing exempt; collection-centre commission booked as a taxable purchase (ITC per Rule 42). | `service-rules.ts` `SERVICE_CLASS_RULES` (`health_exempt`); `services/sv-lab.ts` | 2017-07-01 | N 12/2017-CT(R) entry 74, para 2(s) / 2(zg) as quoted in AAR Kerala (secondary) |
| SV2 ★ | **Cosmetic procedures.** Para 2(zg) excludes hair transplant and cosmetic or plastic surgery, except to restore anatomy / functions affected by congenital defects, developmental abnormalities, injury or trauma. Are dermatology peels, laser hair reduction, Botox, teeth whitening, veneers and aligners "cosmetic" (taxable at 18%)? Which SAC? | The user classes each procedure: `health_cosmetic` at the item's rate (default 18%); reconstructive work stays `health_exempt`. | `SERVICE_CLASS_RULES` (`health_cosmetic`); `services/sv-tax.ts` | 2017-07-01 | Para 2(zg) as quoted (secondary) |
| SV3 ★ | **Hospital rooms.** From 18 Jul 2022 a room (other than ICU / CCU / ICCU / NICU) with a charge above ₹5,000 per day per patient is taxed at 5% without ITC (SAC 9993). The app tests the **room charge alone** (not nursing or other per-day charges), per day per patient, counts room-days by midnight census, treats exactly ₹5,000 as exempt and a day-care bed as a room. Is each of these right? Is the 5% on the room line only, with the rest of the admission exempt? | As stated. | `service-rules.ts` `HOSPITAL_ROOM_RULES`; `services/sv-ipd.ts` `roomClass` | 2022-07-18 | N 03/2022-CT(R) entry 31A; N 04/2022-CT(R) amending entry 74 (Grant Thornton, taxguru — secondary) |
| SV4 | **In-patient medicines, consumables, implants and food** are part of the composite health care supply (exempt); medicines sold to out-patients from the clinic's pharmacy are goods at their own rates. Is that still right, including for day-care and for a separate pharmacy entity inside the hospital? Must ITC on medicines used for in-patients be reversed (Rule 42)? | Exempt on admissions; goods rate at the counter; ITC on in-patient medicines reversed through the Rule 42 engine. | `services/sv-ipd.ts`; `institutions/common-credit.ts` | — | Circular 32/06/2018-GST (recalled, not re-read) |

For a freelancer the TDS suggestion (194H / 194J) is on the commission **excluding** tips; for an employee the payroll earning includes them (salary TDS u/s 192). Are tips outside the 194H / 194J base, and should they be credited to a "Tips payable" liability instead of sales? | As stated. | `services/sv-commission.ts` (`Statement.tips`); `services/sv-payouts.ts` `postPayout` | — | — (design) |

## 11. Construction (Round 11, ids CN1–CN35)

Added with `docs/prd/round-11.md`. It covers civil, EPC and road contractors (government and private),
sub-contractors, builders and real-estate developers, interiors and MEP contractors, and labour contractors.

- These are **planned** behaviours. The files named under "Where" are the ones Round 11 will create
  (`src/lib/construction-rules.ts`, `src/lib/construction/cn-*.ts`) or the existing works files it extends.
- Ids use the prefix `CN` so they do not clash with other rounds' ids. Points already asked in §5 "Works contracts"
  (I15–I19) are not repeated; CN1 and CN5 extend I15 and I19 with what has changed since.
- Some items are also questions for someone other than a CA: CN15 and CN16 for a labour-law adviser, CN25 for a RERA
  lawyer, and the contract-practice defaults in CN7 and CN8 for the contract itself. Please say so where that applies.
- Raw sources: the round-11 research notes (P = primary text read, S = secondary).

### 11.1 Works-contract GST and the bill

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CN1 ★ | **Works-contract rates from 22 Sep 2025.** All sources agree that N 15/2025-CT(R) moved Government earthwork works (earthwork > 75% of value), offshore oil-and-gas works and their sub-contractors from 12% to 18%. Sources **conflict** on the other 12% items of heading 9954 (Government civil works for non-commercial use, roads, bridges, railways / metro, irrigation and water / sewerage works, PMAY and low-cost houses, and their sub-contractors): moved to 18%, or still 12%? Which items are still 12%, if any? | Every Government works class at 18% from 22 Sep 2025 (as today); the class picker shows "sources conflict — verify"; a typed rate always wins. | `transport-works-rules.ts` `WORKS_CONTRACT_RATE_RULES`; `construction-rules.ts` `WORKS_CLASS_RULES` | 2025-09-22 | taxo.online summary of N 15/2025 (narrow change); busy.in SAC 9954, disytax, casurana (all to 18%) — secondary, conflicting |
| CN2 | **Pure labour.** Are pure labour contracts for a single residential unit (not in a residential complex) and for PMAY beneficiary-led houses still exempt (N 12/2017-CT(R) entries 10A / 10B as recalled)? Is labour with small consumables (nails, binding wire) still "pure labour"? | Class `pure_labour_exempt` prints "Exempt — pure labour"; the user picks it per line. | `construction-rules.ts` `WORKS_CLASS_RULES`; `construction/cn-tax.ts` | 2017-07-01 | busy.in SAC 9954 (secondary); notification recalled |
| CN3 ★ | **Works contract or supply of goods?** Sec 2(119) covers work on **immovable property**. Which MEP and interiors jobs are works contracts (18% on the whole) and which are a supply of goods with installation (goods rate; installation a separate or ancillary service): split ACs, central HVAC, lifts, modular kitchens, wardrobes, false ceilings, solar plants, electrical wiring, fire-fighting systems? Does showing supply and installation on separate lines change the answer? | Lines keep their own classes (`supply_goods` at the HSN rate, `install_service` at 18%); a caution offers "treat the whole as a works contract". | `construction/cn-tax.ts` | — | CGST Act Sec 2(119), Sch II para 6(a) (recalled) |
| CN4 ★ | **Place of supply and registration.** For services directly in relation to immovable property the place of supply is the site (Sec 12(3)). (a) A contractor registered only in State A doing a site in State B: IGST from State A, or must it register in State B? (b) Does a site office or store make it a fixed establishment there? (c) A site in two States (a highway): how is the value split? | PoS = site State; a notice when no sender profile is registered there; IGST / CGST + SGST by the existing PoS logic; no split for multi-State sites (the user splits bills). | `construction/cn-tax.ts` | — | IGST Act Sec 12(3) (recalled) |
| CN5 | **Labour cess under the Social Security Code.** From 21 Nov 2025 the BOCW cess sits in the Code on Social Security 2020 Sec 100 (1–2%), with 1% notified by S.O. 2322(E) dated 8 May 2026 (as reported). (a) Same 1%, same deduction at source by Government / PSU / local authority? (b) The base is the cost of construction excluding land (Cess Rules 1998 Rule 3); a Kerala HC ruling (19 Aug 2026, as reported) excludes the supply component of a composite contract. Should the app deduct on the gross before GST (as today) or on a "construction cost only" base? Extends I19. | Same 1% with a new dated row; base "value before GST", with a per-contract option "construction cost only". | `transport-works-rules.ts` `LABOUR_CESS_RULES`; `works/deductions.ts` | 2025-11-21 | taxmann, barandbench, labour.delhi.gov.in, supremetoday (secondary) |
| CN6 ★ | **Price variation / escalation.** (a) A positive price variation under the contract (CPWD clause 10CC-type) is further consideration for the same works contract: taxed at the contract's rate, on its own invoice or as a line of the next RA bill? (b) Time of supply: the date the variation is invoiced / certified? (c) A negative variation: a Sec 34 credit note, and what if the time limit has passed? (d) Does GST TDS u/s 51 apply to an escalation invoice? | (a) Own invoice or RA line, at the contract's class rate. (b) Invoice date. (c) Credit note with a time-limit warning. (d) Same deduction rules as the RA bill. | `construction/cn-escalation.ts` | — | Sec 13, 15, 34 CGST Act (developers' reading) |
| CN7 ★ | **Secured advance.** An advance paid by the client against materials brought to site (CPWD GCC clause 10B; 75% of the assessed value in most sources, 90% per CAG), recovered as the materials are used. Is it an advance for the supply (GST at receipt, Sec 13(2)) or a loan secured on the materials (no GST)? Does the contractor's hypothecation of the materials to the client matter? | Liability "Secured advance from clients", no GST at receipt; recovered from RA bills as the material is consumed. | `construction/cn-secured.ts`; `construction-rules.ts` `ADVANCE_DEFAULTS` | — | CAG Report 35/2015 ch. 7; CPWD practice (secondary) |
| CN8 | **Interest on mobilisation advance.** The client charges simple interest (CPWD practice: 10%) on the unrecovered advance and deducts it from RA bills. Is this interest a supply to the contractor (exempt as interest) or a reduction of the contract value? Is it a deductible finance cost? Any TDS by the contractor (194A does not apply to Government payees)? | A deduction kind `advance_interest`, no GST, booked as "Interest on mobilisation advance" (finance cost); no TDS. | `construction/cn-advances.ts` | — | CPWD payments practice (secondary); developers' reading |
| CN9 ★ | **Materials issued by the client.** (a) Free-issue materials (cement / steel given without charge): outside the contractor's value of supply (the reasoning of Commissioner v Bhayana Builders, SC 2018, under service tax), or included under Sec 15(2)(b)? (b) Materials issued **against recovery** at a fixed rate: a supply by the client to the contractor (GST invoice from the client, ITC to the contractor), or simply a reduction of the bill? (c) Excess wastage recovered at a penal rate: what is it for GST? | (a) Quantity only, not in value. (b) Booked as a purchase from the client at the recovery value; ITC only if the client issues a GST invoice. (c) An expense, no GST. | `construction/cn-recoveries.ts`, `cn-reco.ts` | — | Bhayana Builders (SC 2018, recalled); Sec 15 CGST Act |
| CN10 | **Water and electricity recovered by the client** (commonly 1% + 1% of the bill). Electricity is exempt; water is mostly exempt. Is the recovery a supply by the client, a reimbursement, or a reduction of consideration? Any ITC point for the contractor? | Site expense "Water & electricity — site", no GST, no ITC. | `construction/cn-recoveries.ts` | — | Developers' reading |
| CN11 ★ | **Liquidated damages.** Under Circular 178/10/2022-GST, LD deducted by the client for delay is compensation for breach, not consideration: no GST on it. (a) Does the LD reduce the taxable value of the RA / final bill (so the contractor's GST was overpaid), or does GST stay on the full bill value with the LD as an expense? (b) Is LD deductible for income tax (compensation, not a penalty for an offence)? (c) LD later waived and refunded: income in the year of refund? | GST on the full value; LD booked as an expense "Liquidated damages"; a refund is income when received. | `works/deductions.ts`; `construction/cn-recoveries.ts` | 2022-08-03 | Circular 178/10/2022-GST (EY, Grant Thornton, Bar & Bench — secondary) |
| CN12 | **Retention and withheld amounts in income tax.** Retention money and amounts withheld by the client: revenue in the year of the bill (ICDS III; Sec 43CB) or in the year of release? When the client deducts 194C on the retention only at release, how should the TDS credit be matched? | Revenue at the bill (the bill is issued for the full value); TDS credit matched when it appears in 26AS / AIS. | `works/ra-due.ts`; `construction/cn-site-pnl.ts` | — | ICDS III / Sec 43CB (recalled) |
| CN13 | **Bank guarantees, EMD and security deposits.** BG commission charged by the bank: 18% with ITC? Margin-money FD: an asset with taxable interest? EMD and SD paid to the client: not a supply, no GST? EMD forfeited by the client: an expense, any GST on the forfeiture? | As stated; forfeiture is an expense with no GST. | `construction/cn-guarantees.ts`; `property/deposits.ts` | — | Developers' reading |

(c) Any exemption for labour supplied to a Government department? | (a) As stated. (b) A warning and a proposed hold, never a block. (b) Wages paid weekly from a site muster without payroll: any TDS (192 rarely applies at these wages)? (c) Minimum wages under the Code on Wages: is a warning against a typed rate per trade enough? | Warning above ₹10,000 per person per day; no TDS; minimum-wage warning. | `construction/cn-muster.ts` | — | Sec 40A(3) (recalled) |

(b) GST on hiring out: with an operator 18% (which SAC, 9966 or 9973?); without an operator the rate of the goods? (c) Internal charge of our own plant to our own site: no supply. | As stated; SAC from `PLANT_HIRE_SAC_RULES` marked "verify". | `construction/cn-plant-hire.ts`; `tds-rules.ts` | — | Developers' reading |

(b) Material we issued and recover from his bill, and our plant hire recovered from him, credited to recovery ledgers (reducing site cost) without GST unless the user invoices them — acceptable? (a) Which items are excluded (TDR / FSI, long-term lease premium, electricity, HSD, petrol, natural gas)? (b) Is the shortfall paid by 30 June after the FY end? (c) Is the 80% on value excluding tax? | As stated; a working paper and a journal draft, never posted automatically. | `construction-rules.ts` `REALTY_PROCUREMENT_RULES`; `construction/cn-procurement80.ts` | 2019-04-01 | taxguru, AIFTP journal (secondary) |

(c) Which date counts — the CC issue date, the date applied for, or the occupancy certificate? (d) Any ITC reversal for unsold units at CC (old-option projects with ITC)? | (a), (b) As stated. Is that the right reading of "location of the supplier and place of supply in a State different from the State of registration of the recipient"? ## 12. Agriculture and food processing (Round 12, ids AG1–AG45, AG50–AG53, AG60–AG69, AG80–AG84)

Added with `docs/prd/round-12.md`. It covers farmer producer organisations and co-operatives, dairies and milk
collection centres, rice / flour / dal / oil mills, cold storages, seed / fertiliser / pesticide dealers, and poultry /
fishery farms.

- These are **planned** behaviours. The files named under "Where" are the ones Round 12 will create
  (`src/lib/agri-rules.ts`, `src/lib/agri/ag-*.ts`) or the existing files it reuses.
- Ids use the prefix `AG` so they do not clash with other rounds' ids. Points already asked elsewhere are not repeated:
  mandi-fee value (TR6), charges (TR7), aadat on agricultural produce (TR9), RCM notified goods (TR17), 194Q under the
  2025 Act (TR13), GTA (I10) and commercial rent RCM (I28). AG items add only what is new for these businesses.
- **Rates from 22 Sep 2025 (AG4)**: the notification numbers were not read; the app ships every changed rate as a
  dated class **suggestion** marked unverified, and a typed rate always wins.
- Some items are also questions for someone other than a CA: AG19 for the Registrar of Co-operative Societies, AG21 and
  AG22 for the agriculture department, AG24 for a lawyer (money-lending), AG23 for WDRA. Please say so where that
  applies.
- Raw sources: the round-12 research notes (P = primary text read, S = secondary, V = vendor).

### 12.1 GST on produce, dairy, inputs and storage

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AG1 ★ | **"Agricultural produce" and the processes that keep it.** The app treats as agricultural produce (Explanation to N 11/2017-CT(R)): paddy, wheat, pulses whole, potatoes, onions, fresh fruit and vegetables, raw milk, after cleaning, drying, grading, sorting, cooling and bagging by a cultivator or an FPO. It treats as **no longer** agricultural produce: rice (milled), dal (split / polished), flour, ginned cotton, jaggery, processed milk products. (a) Is that line right? (b) Does an FPO's grading / cleaning / packing for members keep the produce "agricultural produce" for the entry 54 services and for storage (AG8)? | As stated; the class list in `AGRI_CLASS_RULES` decides the suggested rate. | `agri-rules.ts` `AGRI_CLASS_RULES`; `agri/ag-tax.ts` | 2017-07-01 | N 11/2017, 12/2017 Explanation (developers' reading) |
| AG2 | **Exempt sales documents.** A sale of only nil-rated produce is a bill of supply (Rule 49); a mixed bill is an invoice-cum-bill of supply (Rule 46A). Nil / exempt values go to GSTR-1 Table 8 and 3B 3.1(c). No e-invoice for a bill of supply. Is that right for a dairy selling milk (nil) and ghee (5%) on one bill? | As stated (existing behaviour). | `bill-of-supply.ts`; `gst-reports.ts` | — | Rules 46A, 49 |
| AG3 | **Curd, lassi, buttermilk, milk powder after 22 Sep 2025.** Loose curd / lassi / buttermilk nil, pre-packaged and labelled 5% (from 18 Jul 2022); skimmed milk powder 5%. Did the 22 Sep 2025 changes touch any of these? | No change assumed (rows unverified). | `AGRI_CLASS_RULES` (`curd_*`, `milk_powder`) | 2022-07-18 | Summaries silent (secondary) |
| AG4 ★ | **Rates changed on 22 Sep 2025** (§2.3a of the PRD): UHT milk and pre-packaged paneer 5% → nil; ghee, butter, butter oil, cheese, condensed milk → 5% (old rate **12%** per PTI / Taxscan, **18%** per one Tally table — which?); tractors (except road tractors for semi-trailers > 1800 cc) 12% → 5%; tractor tyres / tubes / parts 18% → 5%; harvesters, threshers, composting machines, drip / sprinklers, hand pumps, diesel engines ≤ 15 HP 12% → 5%; sulphuric acid, nitric acid, ammonia 18% → 5%; bio-pesticides, micronutrients, gibberellic acid 12% → 5%. Fertilisers 5%, chemical pesticides 18%, seeds for sowing and cattle / poultry / aquatic feed nil — unchanged. Please confirm each and give the notification numbers. | As stated, every row `verified: false`; typed rate wins. | `AGRI_CLASS_RULES` | 2025-09-22 | Taxscan (56th Council), PTI (The Week), Tally rate table, Ministry of Cooperation (secondary, partly conflicting) |
| AG5 | **Pre-packaged and labelled cereals, pulses, flour.** 5% when pre-packaged and labelled in a package up to 25 kg / 25 L (N 6/2022 from 18 Jul 2022); nil above 25 kg and loose; a retailer's loose sale from a 25 kg bag nil. Unchanged after 22 Sep 2025? Is a 26 kg bag sold to a retailer nil even if labelled? Does a 50 kg bag with the mill's brand printed count? | As stated; the line class follows `ag_pack_kg` and `ag_labelled`. | `agri/ag-packing.ts`; `PACKED_25KG_RULES` | 2022-07-18 | FinMin FAQ 2022 (secondary) |
| AG6 | **Mill by-products.** Rice husk nil (1213); rice bran nil as cattle / poultry / aquatic feed, 5% otherwise (2302); de-oiled rice bran 5% irrespective of use (2306); broken rice: one AAR says 5% (pre-2022 regime) — is loose broken rice nil like other cereals now? Oil cake: nil when supplied as cattle feed (S.No. 102 N 2/2017 as amended 1 Jan 2023), 5% otherwise? What evidence of "end use" is enough (buyer's declaration)? | Class pairs with an end-use choice and a typed buyer declaration; broken rice like other cereals (loose nil / packed 5%). | `AGRI_CLASS_RULES` (`rice_*`, `dorb`, `broken_rice`, `oil_cake_*`) | — | GST Council FAQ (2024 reissue); AAR (Tamal Kundu); AAR on cattle feed |
| AG7 | **Services of an FPO / co-operative** to members: cleaning, grading, sorting, drying, packing, storage, renting of farm machinery, aggregation and sale as commission agent — nil under N 12/2017 entry 54 for agricultural produce? Is the FPO's **own** sale of members' produce (it buys and resells) a supply of goods at the goods' rate (nil for produce)? | Entry 54 services suggested nil (`storage_agri`, `job_milling` excluded); FPO resale of produce = goods at the class rate. | `agri/ag-tax.ts` | 2017-07-01 | N 12/2017 entry 54 (recalled) |
| AG8 ★ | **Cold-storage rent.** (a) Storage of agricultural produce nil (entry 54(e)), whoever the depositor is (AP AAR, SSSVK) — right? (b) Renting a whole chamber / space for a lump sum: a separate taxable supply (Gujarat AAR, 9972, 18%) or still storage (AP AAR, 998619, nil)? (c) Loading / unloading / sorting charges billed with exempt storage: part of the exempt composite supply? (d) Potatoes, onions, fruit and vegetables are agricultural produce; dry fruit, spices, frozen foods, medicines are not (18%) — right? (e) Is unbilled season rent at the year end to be accrued? | (a) Nil; (b) 18% default with a note; (c) follows the storage class; (d) as stated; (e) memo of unbilled rent on the depositor statement, no auto-accrual. | `agri/ag-cold-rent.ts`; `STORAGE_CLASS_RULES` | 2017-07-01 | N 12/2017 entry 54(e); AARs on gstcouncil.gov.in (conflicting) |
| AG9 ★ | **Milling for PDS (custom milling).** Circular 153/09/2021-GST: milling of paddy into rice for the State for PDS is a composite supply of service; exempt (entry 3A) if goods (fortificant, gunny, packing) ≤ 25% of its value; else 5% as job work to a registered person (entry 26, N 11/2017), including a person registered only for Sec 51 TDS. (a) Is the 25% test on our milling-charge invoice value including the by-product value the agency recovers? (b) Is the by-product retained by the miller (and its value recovered by the agency) further consideration for milling? | The editor shows the 25% test from typed values and suggests exempt / 5%; by-product recovery is shown, not added to value. | `agri/ag-cmr.ts`; `MILLING_PDS_RULES` | 2021-06-17 | Circular 153/09/2021 (HP, Goa copies — primary) |
| AG10 | **Job milling for others.** Atta chakki grinding a household's wheat, oil expeller crushing a farmer's mustard, dal milling for a trader: 5% job work (entry 26 "food and food products") when the principal is registered; for an unregistered farmer / household — 18%, 5%, or exempt? | 5% suggested for a registered principal; "verify" and the user's rate otherwise. | `agri/ag-cmr.ts` (`job_milling`) | — | N 11/2017 entry 26 (recalled) |
| AG11 | **RCM on purchases from farmers.** No RCM on milk, paddy, cereals, potatoes, vegetables bought from farmers (exempt, not in N 4/2017); RCM only for the notified goods (TR17). Inward RCM these businesses meet: GTA freight on procurement (I10), legal, security, renting of commercial property by an unregistered person (I28). Is any other notified supply relevant (e.g. services of a commission agent to a cold store)? | As stated. | `trading-rules.ts` (unchanged) | — | N 4/2017, 13/2017-CT(R) (recalled) |
| AG12 | **e-Way bill for produce.** Not required for goods exempt under N 2/2017 (fresh milk, loose cereals, vegetables, paddy) and for the Annexure goods of Rule 138(14) — right? Required for 5% packed rice / atta, oil, de-oiled bran above ₹50,000? For a mixed consignment, is the value test on the taxable part only? | Prompt only for taxable lines above the threshold; exempt-only bills show a "may not be needed — verify" note. | `agri/ag-packing.ts`; existing e-way JSON | — | Rule 138(14) (recalled) |

### 12.2 TDS, cash and income tax

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AG13 | **194Q on purchases from farmers.** A mill / FPO with turnover > ₹10 crore buying paddy from one farmer above ₹50 lakh in a FY deducts 0.1% (agricultural produce is "goods"); 206C(1H) does not apply from 1 Apr 2025. With pooled farmer cycles (AG26) the ₹50 lakh is tested **per farmer** from the cycle lines. Right, and the same under the 2025 Act (TR13)? | As stated; a prompt, never automatic. | `agri/ag-vouchers.ts`; `trading-rules.ts` `BUYER_194Q_TURNOVER_RULES` | 2021-07-01; 2025-04-01 | incometaxindia.gov.in (194Q page, primary); ICAI note on Finance Act 2025 |
| AG14 ★ | **Cash to farmers (Sec 40A(3) / Rule 6DD(e)).** Cash paid to the cultivator / grower / producer of agricultural produce, dairy or poultry products, fish, horticulture or apiculture products is outside 40A(3). (a) Does it cover a cash payment of a pooled milk cycle where each farmer's share is ≤ or > ₹10,000? (b) Payment through a collection-centre agent who then pays the farmers? (c) What is the 2025 Act's provision and rule number? (d) The Madras HC (2025) reads "producer" as the farmer only — so cash to a dairy company or a trader stays disallowed; right? | No 40A(3) warning for agriculturist vendors and pooled farmer cycles, with a note to keep the farmer's acknowledgement; the warning stays for companies / traders / processors. | `agri/ag-cash.ts` (called by `rt-cash-guard.ts`, `cn-muster.ts`); `RULE_6DDE_RULES` | — | Rule 6DD (incometaxindia.gov.in, primary); Arasappan Madhivanan v ITO (Mad HC 2025, secondary report) |
| AG15 | **Agricultural income.** Sec 10(1) (1961) → Sec 11 + Schedule II Table S.No. 1 (2025 Act) as per mapping tables. The app treats only own-cultivation income as agricultural; income of mills, dairies, cold stores, FPOs (buying and selling), poultry and fishery is business income. Right? Is income from a nursery or from sale of saplings grown on land agricultural? | As stated; a ledger "Agricultural income (exempt)" for the farm kind only; no tax computation. | `agri/ag-profile.ts`; help | 2026-04-01 (label) | taxheal / cleartax mapping (secondary) — Act text not read |
| AG16 ★ | **Co-operative deduction (80P → Sec 149 of the 2025 Act).** The working paper splits income by head: marketing members' agricultural produce, supplying agricultural inputs to members, milk supplied to a federal milk co-op, credit to members, other. (a) Is Sec 149 the right home and are the heads the same? (b) Is income from non-members (patronage register shows it) outside the deduction? (c) Interest from a co-operative bank deposit (contested in ITATs)? | Labels by date; figures by head from the books and the patronage register; nothing reaches a return. | `agri/ag-coop-papers.ts`; `COOP_DEDUCTION_RULES` | 2026-04-01 | taxguru (ITA 2025 co-op note, secondary); incometaxindia 80P page |
| AG17 ★ | **Producer company deduction (80PA).** 100% of eligible-business profits for a producer company with turnover < ₹100 crore, **AY 2019-20 to AY 2024-25 only**. We found no extension and no section of the 2025 Act carrying it (the Bill's clause 150 became a definitions section). Has it been extended or re-enacted? | No deduction computed for AY 2025-26 onward; the paper says "none found — verify". | `agri/ag-coop-papers.ts`; `PRODUCER_COMPANY_RULES` | 2025-04-01 | taxguru, studycafe, taxheal; taxtmi notes (secondary) |

(a) Is the fee on paddy bought **outside** a market yard (direct purchase from the farm) still due in States that allow it? (a) Must the dealer's GST invoice match the PoS quantity and price exactly? | Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AG26 | **Pooled farmer bills.** A milk centre with 200 farmers records **one purchase voucher per cycle** (vendor "Milk producers — <centre>") with the farmer-wise split on the cycle row, instead of 7,000 individual bills a year. Farmer balances, slips and 194Q per-farmer totals come from the split. Is that acceptable as the books of account (Sec 44AA / Rule 6F) and for audit, given the farmers are unregistered and issue no invoices? | As stated. | `agri/ag-cycles.ts`, `ag-cycle-post.ts` | — | — (design) |
| AG27 | **Feed / input set-off from milk money.** A farmer's cattle-feed or input invoice is settled out of his milk money: a receipt marked as settled against the cycle's purchase voucher, and one journal moving the set-off total from the pooled payable to "Sundry creditors — exchange". Is the presentation right? Any issue with Sec 269ST / 269T for the set-off (no cash)? | As stated. | `agri/ag-cycle-post.ts`; `no-cash.ts` marker `exchange_purchase_id` (unchanged) | — | — (design) |
| AG28 | **Milk stock and shrinkage.** Milk collected is stock in the centre at the shift's purchase value; dispatches and products take it out; the difference between collected and dispatched (kg fat / SNF) is shown as shrinkage. Should normal shrinkage be absorbed in cost (AS 2) and abnormal shrinkage written off separately? Is a tolerance of 0.5% of kg fat a reasonable default for flagging? | Memo only; no automatic write-off journal. | `agri/ag-milk-reco.ts`; `MILK_DEFAULTS` | — | — (practice) |
| AG29 | **Mill joint costs.** By-products (bran, husk, broken) credited at a fixed rate (net realisable value) and co-products costed by a % of joint cost — both existing BOM options. Which should the starters default to for rice (broken as co-product or by-product)? Paddy received for CMR and the agency's gunny are never our stock — right for the stock statement to the bank too? | Broken = co-product (5% of cost), bran / husk = by-products at typed rates; CMR paddy excluded everywhere. | `agri/ag-mill-bom.ts`; `tr-stock-statement.ts` preset | — | AS 2 (developers' reading); CAG 2015 |
| AG30 ★ | **Cold-storage revenue and custody.** Depositors' goods are never in our stock or balance sheet; rent is billed at release (default), monthly, at season end or in advance. (a) At the year end, should rent earned but not billed (goods still in store) be accrued as income? (b) Is insurance taken by the cold store on depositors' goods recoverable as part of rent (composite) or a pure-agent reimbursement? | (a) Memo of unbilled rent shown; no auto-accrual; (b) part of rent (composite) by default. | `agri/ag-cold-rent.ts` | — | — |
| AG31 | **Poultry and fishery.** Live poultry, eggs and fresh fish nil; frozen / packed meat and fish 5%? Feed for poultry / fish nil. Is poultry or fish farming income ever agricultural income (AG15 says no)? Any TDS on purchases of chicks / fingerlings (none assumed)? | As stated; batches with FCR are a memo on a cost centre. | `AGRI_CLASS_RULES` (`live_poultry`, `eggs`, `fish_fresh`); `agri/ag-batches.ts` | — | Recalled |
| AG32 | **Credit till harvest.** A dealer selling seeds (nil) and fertiliser (5%) on credit due at harvest charges interest after the due date: interest for delayed payment is part of the value (Sec 15(2)(d), G6), so nil on seeds and 5% on fertiliser — right? On a mixed bill, is the interest split by line value? | Interest notes take each bill's line rates (existing TR18 behaviour), split by line value on a mixed bill. | `trade/tr-interest.ts` (season due date); `agri/ag-season-credit.ts` | — | Sec 15(2)(d) (developers' reading) |

### 12.5 Added while building AG-A (profile, farmers, vouchers, cycles, paper)

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AG33 | **Where the feed set-off lands in the books.** The app posts a set-off receipt (`exchange_purchase_id`) as Dr Sundry creditors (party = the farmer) / Cr Debtors (the R9 posting rule), so the cycle's journal is Dr pooled vendor / Cr Sundry creditors (party = the farmer) — not a separate "Sundry creditors — exchange" ledger as AG27 described. Is this presentation acceptable (both sides clear inside Sundry creditors)? | As stated. | `agri/ag-cycle-post.ts` `closeCycle`; `ledger/postings.ts` (unchanged) | — | — (design) |
| AG34 | **Quality cuts and recovered charges on the voucher value.** A moisture / foreign-matter cut reduces the accepted weight (line quantity) or the rate; hamali / bardana recovered from the farmer is a less-line on the voucher. So the GSTR-3B Table 5 exempt-inward value is the net voucher value. Should Table 5 show the gross produce value instead, with the recovered charges as our income? | Net voucher value. | `agri/ag-vouchers.ts`; `gst-reports.ts` Table 5 (unchanged) | — | — (design) |
| AG35 | **Mandi fee paid by the buyer.** Market fee / RDF / commission "paid by us" are printed on the farmer voucher as a memo only; the user books them as an expense (or to the mandi payable). Is it right that they are not part of the purchase value of the produce (stock cost)? Should they be capitalised into the stock cost (AS 2)? | Memo on the voucher; booked separately as an expense. | `agri/ag-vouchers.ts` (charges `by: 'us'`) | — | AS 2 (developers' reading) |
| AG36 | **Rule 6DD(e) on a pooled cash payment.** A cycle's cash farmers are paid by one cash payment on the pooled bill (the total may exceed ₹10,000 although each farmer's share is smaller). The app shows the Rule 6DD(e) note, never the Sec 40A(3) warning, for payments to a farmer vendor or a pooled farmer cycle. Is the per-farmer split on the cycle (with each farmer's acknowledgement) enough evidence? | As stated. | `agri/ag-cash.ts`; `retail/rt-cash-guard.ts` `cashPaymentIssue` | 2017-04-01 | IT Rules 1962 Rule 6DD(e) |
| AG37 | **A farmer's net below zero.** When deductions exceed a farmer's milk / produce money, the net is paid as zero and the excess is carried to his next cycle as a deduction (memo on the cycle rows; no debtor is booked in between). Is it acceptable that the receivable sits only on the cycle until the next close, or must it be booked as a debtor at the cycle end (year end at least)? | Memo carry-forward; reviewed at year end. | `agri/ag-cycles.ts` `lineNet`, `carriedIn` | — | — (design) |
| AG38 | **Bonus / price difference run.** The year-end run computes ₹ per unit on each farmer's cycle quantity and exports a CSV; the journal (price difference to purchase cost, or patronage to appropriation, AG25) is entered by the user. Should the run post a draft journal itself, and should the bonus be spread over the farmers' bills of the year (for 194Q totals)? | CSV working only; journal by the user. | `agri/ag-cycles.ts` `bonusRun`; Farm → Cycles | — | — (design) |
| AG39 | **Farm batches and agricultural income.** A flock / pond / herd / crop batch is a cost centre with a memo (mortality %, FCR, cost per kg); no posting from events. The "Agricultural income (exempt)" ledger is offered only for own cultivation. Is a separate ledger enough for the return (Schedule EI), or should exempt agricultural income also be kept out of the P&L totals the app shows? | Separate ledger under Other income; P&L shows it. | `agri/ag-batches.ts`; `agri/ag-cycle-post.ts` `agriIncomeLedger` | — | IT Act 1961 Sec 10(1) / 2025 Act Sec 11 (as reported, AG15) |

(a) Khoa / mawa — which heading and rate (0402 / 0406; nil, 5% or 12% before 22 Sep 2025)? | AG50 | **Paddy received for custom milling (CMR) as the agency's material.** Each paddy lot is a job-work principal's receipt (JWP register, never our stock); each rice delivery (CMR/FY…, against the agency's acknowledgement) is recorded as a return of the paddy it was milled from (rice ÷ outturn %, marked processed). (a) Is that register enough as our record of the agency's goods (Sec 143 / Rule 45 apply to the principal; anything for the miller)? (b) A shortfall in outturn (rice delivered below 67 / 68% of paddy) that the agency recovers in money — a supply by the miller, a penalty, or a reduction of the milling charges? (c) Gunny / FRK issued by the agency: register only? | (a) Register only; (b) not computed — shown as rice still due; (c) typed as principal's material lines. | `agri/ag-cmr.ts` (`addPaddyLot`, `addDelivery`); `job-work.ts` `principalDb` (unchanged) | 2021-06-17 | Circular 153/09/2021 (primary); job-work provisions (developers' reading) |
| AG51 ★ | **The 25% test per milling-charges bill.** The app runs the Circular 153 test on each bill: goods typed on the bill (fortificant / FRK bought by the miller, gunny, packing) ÷ (milling charges + those goods); ≤ 25% → exempt (entry 3A), else 5% (entry 26). (a) Is the test per bill right, or per agreement / season / State order? (b) FRK supplied by the agency (not charged by us) — left out of both values? (c) By-product value the agency recovers — left out (as AG9 assumes)? | Per bill; agency-supplied goods and by-product recovery left out; the user decides. | `agri/ag-cmr.ts` `pdsTest`, `millingChargesInvoice`; `MILLING_PDS_RULES` | 2021-06-17 | Circular 153/09/2021 (primary) |
| AG52 | **Milling loss and moisture loss.** A batch shows loss = input − all outputs, split into moisture loss (input × (m_in − m_out) ÷ (100 − m_out)) and an unexplained part. Both stay in the cost of the main product and co-products (normal loss, AS 2); nothing is written off and no journal is made. Should an unexplained loss above a tolerance be treated as abnormal and charged to P&L, and is a 1-point outturn tolerance a reasonable flag? | Memo and flag only; normal loss absorbed. | `agri/ag-yield.ts`; `OUTTURN_STANDARDS` | — | AS 2 (developers' reading); practice |
| AG53 | **Bank stock statement for a mill.** The drawing-power working groups stock as paddy / rice / broken / bran / husk / packing at the books' cost (by-products at the typed rate the BOM credits), deducts creditors for goods, and keeps CMR / job-milling paddy out (memo of quintals held). Is valuing by-products at that rate acceptable for a bank's stock statement, and should paddy bought but not yet paid (farmer vouchers) be deducted like other creditors? | As stated; margins typed per account. | `agri/ag-mill-reports.ts`; `trade/tr-stock-statement.ts` (`STOCK_STATEMENT_PRESETS.mill`) | — | Bank practice (TR19) |

### 12.8 Cold storage, input dealers and members — choices made while building AG-D (AG60–AG69)

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AG60 | **Bags transferred inside the store.** A depositor sells bags to another depositor while they are in store. The receiver gets a new receipt carrying the original date in, and rent for the whole period is billed to whoever takes the bags out. Should the seller instead be billed up to the transfer date, and does the transfer need its own document? | The receiver's child lot keeps the original date in; rent follows the plan and goes to the depositor taking the bags out; both parties' consent note is kept on the lot. | `agri/ag-cold-lots.ts` `transferBags` | — | — (practice) |
| AG61 ★ | **Time of supply of season rent.** Season rent is billed when bags are released (the default), in advance, monthly or at the season end. Storage over a season looks like a continuous supply of services (Sec 31(5), Rule 47). (a) Must a taxable storage bill (18%) be raised by the due date of payment or every month even when the plan says "at release"? (b) For exempt storage, is a bill of supply at release enough? | A draft bill is raised at the time the plan names; the user may bill a period at any time. Exempt storage gets a bill of supply. | `agri/ag-cold-rent.ts` `rentOccasion`, `raiseRentBill` | 2017-07-01 | CGST Act Sec 31(5); Rule 47 (developers' reading) |
| AG62 ★ | **Loan interest basis.** Loans against lots use simple interest by days on a 365-day year from disbursement or the last repayment, applied interest first. A "monthly compound" option adds unpaid interest to the base at each month start. (a) Do State money-lenders laws bar compound interest or cap the rate for loans to farmers? (b) Is interest-first allocation acceptable? | Simple interest by default; compounding only if the user picks it; a note says to verify with a lawyer (AG24). | `agri/ag-loans.ts` `loanState`, `splitRepayment` | — | — (practice; AG24) |
| AG63 | **Late rent and handling charges.** Rent after the season ends (per bag per month) and loading / unloading charges are billed at the plan's storage class: nil for agricultural produce, 18% otherwise. Is late rent a part of the same exempt storage supply? | As stated; a charge may carry its own rate ("own rate") if the user decides it is a separate supply (AG8 (c)). | `agri/ag-cold-rent.ts` `rentFor` | — | N 12/2017 entry 54(e) (developers' reading) |
| AG64 | **Release under a lien.** Bags under a loan are blocked at the gate pass. A partial release is allowed when the bags left still cover the amount due at a typed loan-to-value % and market rate per bag. The owner may override with a reason that goes to the audit log. Is that a sound control? | As stated; no automatic sale of pledged goods. | `agri/ag-gatepass.ts`, `ag-loans.ts` `releaseCovered` | — | — (practice) |
| AG65 | **Subsidised fertiliser cap.** The 50-bags-a-month warning counts bags on the farmer's issued bills in the calendar month, matched by customer and farmer ID (Aadhaar last 4 or KCC). Is a calendar month right, or a rolling 30 days? Is matching on last 4 digits plus the customer enough? | Calendar month; a warning only, never a block. | `agri/ag-subsidy.ts` `capWarning` | — | Reported practice (AG22) |
| AG66 | **Credit till harvest due date.** A season due date typed as month-day (e.g. Kharif 15 Nov) falls on the next such day on or after the sale. Interest notes start from that date even when the company counts interest from bill date plus credit days. Is that right? | As stated (`tr-interest.ts` uses the season due date first). | `agri/ag-season-credit.ts`; `trade/tr-interest.ts` | — | — (practice) |
| AG67 | **Share capital entries.** Allotment from application money is a journal (Dr "Share application money — members", Cr "Share capital — members"). A surrender is refunded at face value. A forfeiture is credited to "Forfeited shares" under reserves. (a) May a producer company's member surrender shares for a refund, or only transfer them (Sec 378 provisions as recalled)? (b) Is the forfeiture entry right for co-operatives? | As stated; certificates are numbered `SHC/FY…` and a transfer gets a new certificate. | `agri/ag-members.ts` | 2021-02-11 | Companies Act Ch. XXIA (as recalled; AG18, AG19) |
| AG68 | **Patronage basis and the profit split.** A member's business is the value supplied (cycle amounts and the member's own vouchers at taxable value) plus the value bought (sales at taxable value). The split runs: statutory reserve % on profit after tax, then dividend at the lower of the typed % and the cap on paid-up capital, then the patronage pool shared by business, and the rest retained. Is that the right order and basis? | A draft (optional) journal only, for the general meeting. | `agri/ag-patronage.ts` `patronage`, `distribution` | — | — (practice; AG18, AG25) |
| AG69 | **80P / Sec 149 members' share.** Next to each head of profit the paper shows the members' share of supplies from the patronage register, and a "members' part" (profit × that share). Is that a sensible way to apportion income from members vs non-members? | Shown as a memo; the CA decides the claim. | `agri/ag-coop-papers.ts` `coopPaper` | 2026-04-01 | — (AG16) |

### 12.9 Raised by QA round 13 (`docs/prd/qa-round-13.md`)

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AG80 | **A farmer whose deductions exceed his milk money (QA13 G1).** His line nets to zero and the excess carries to the next cycle. The deductions journal debits the pooled producers' account with the full deductions, so that account shows a debit (the farmer owes us) until the next cycle recovers it; the pooled bill is marked settled except for the farmers' net. Is a debit balance on the pooled account acceptable, or should the carried amount move to the farmer's own receivable (or "Farmer advances") at close? | Debit on the pooled account; the cycle row holds the farmer-wise carry. | `agri/ag-cycle-post.ts` `closeCycle` | — | — (design) |
| AG81 ★ | **Loose sale from a labelled bag (QA13 G6).** The 25 kg rule is applied to editor / POS lines from the stock item's pack size and label. A line selling less than one pack (e.g. 5 kg from a labelled 25 kg rice bag) is treated as a loose counter sale (nil); whole packs ≤ 25 kg as pre-packaged and labelled (5%). Is "less than one pack" the right test, and does a retailer's sale of a whole labelled 25 kg bag stay at 5% (FinMin FAQ of July 2022)? | As stated; a soft notice, the typed rate wins. | `agri/ag-hooks.ts`, `ag-packing.ts` `packClass` | 2022-07-18 | N 6/2022-CT(R); FinMin FAQ (2022) — as recalled |
| AG82 | **e-Way bill for nil / exempt goods (QA13 G5).** When every goods line of a bill is at nil, the e-Way preflight shows "may not be needed for exempt goods — verify (AG12)". Rule 138(14) lists goods in its Annexure and goods exempt under N 2/2017-CT(R) (except de-oiled cake); nil-rated goods outside that notification still need one. Is a note on any all-nil bill acceptable, or should it be limited to N 2/2017 goods (by HSN)? | Note on any all-nil bill; nothing is blocked. | `einvoice-validate.ts` (`EWB_EXEMPT`); `agri/ag-packing.ts` `ewayHint` | 2018-04-01 | Rule 138(14) CGST Rules (as recalled) |
| AG83 | **Milk sold to the union: stock vs bill (QA13 G4).** Stock leaves at the sent litres (the dispatch's stock journal); the bill to the union is on its received figures (kg, fat, SNF) and moves no stock; the transit difference is a memo in the reconciliation, absorbed in the cost of milk sold. Right, or should a transit loss above tolerance be written off separately? | As stated. | `agri/ag-dispatch.ts`, `inventory.ts`, `ag-milk-reco.ts` | — | AS 2 (developers' reading) |
| AG84 | **194Q with pooled cycles (QA13 G14).** A farmer's purchases for the 194Q threshold now add his amounts on closed pooled cycles (paid through the centre's pooled bill) to his own vouchers in the FY. Is the per-farmer split the right base, given the pooled bill is booked in the centre's name? | Per farmer, cycle lines + own vouchers, by the cycle's end date. | `agri/ag-vouchers.ts` `fyPurchasesOf` | 2021-07-01 | Sec 194Q (2025-Act label from `ita2025.ts`) |
| AG90 | **Allotment from share application money (QA13 follow-up).** An allotment paid from application money is now refused above what is available on its date: the credit balance of "Share application money — members" is the ceiling; a member's own share deductions from pooled cycles are his, and only the rest of the ledger (receipts not tied to a cycle) is open to any member. Is that attribution right (e.g. should a member's own application receipt be tagged to him), and should money held beyond the period allowed for allotment be shown as refundable? | As stated. | `agri/ag-members.ts` `allotShares`, `applicationAvailable` | — | Companies Act 2013 Sec 42 / Sec 378A (producer companies) — as recalled |

## 13. Manufacturing MSMEs (Round 13, ids MF1–MF36, MF40–MF42, MF50–MF54, MF60–MF65, MF80–MF82, MF90–MF96)

Added with `docs/prd/round-13.md`. It covers engineering / fabrication job shops, textile and garment units, plastics and
packaging converters, printing presses and small food / FMCG units, plus what every factory meets: scrap sales, job-work
e-way bills and ITC-04, MSME (Udyam, 43B(h), Form-1), export authorisations, incentives and inventory valuation.

- These are **planned** behaviours. The files named under "Where" are the ones Round 13 will create
  (`src/lib/mfg-rules.ts`, `src/lib/mfg/mf-*.ts`) or the existing files it reuses.
- Ids use the prefix `MF`. Points already asked elsewhere are not repeated: garment / footwear price bands (RT1, RT2),
  TCS presets on specified goods (TR14), job-work charges and ITC-04 frequency (job-work items in §1.4), Sec 143 clocks,
  export realisation and RoDTEP accrual (X1–X3), batch genealogy (I36), MSME payables 43B(h) arithmetic (the existing
  `msme.ts` items). MF items add only what is new for factories.
- **Rates from 22 Sep 2025**: the notification texts (N 9/2025-CT(R), N 15/2025-CT(R)) were not read; every changed rate
  ships as a dated class **suggestion** marked unverified, and a typed rate always wins.
- Some items are also questions for someone other than a CA: MF13 / MF14 for an FSSAI consultant, MF16 / MF17 for a DGFT
  consultant, MF27 for an EPR (environment) consultant, MF28 for a labour-law adviser. Please say so where that applies.
- Raw sources: the round-13 research notes (P = primary text read, S = secondary, V = vendor).

### 13.1 GST on manufactured goods, job work and scrap

Unchanged after 22 Sep 2025? Same rate when the principal is unregistered? | 5% suggested; typed rate wins. | `mfg/mf-cmt.ts` (`jw_textile`) | 2017-10-13 (as recalled) | fibre2fashion, CITI (2017 reports) |

(d) The seller records the TDS as "GST TDS receivable" on the receipt and claims it after GSTR-7 — right? | As stated; prompts only. | `mfg/mf-scrap-tax.ts`; `SCRAP_CLASS_RULES` | 2024-10-10 | vjmglobal; taxroutine; taxscan (GSTN advisory) |
