## 14. Professional and knowledge-services firms (Round 14, ids PS1–PS44, PS50–PS56, PS60–PS64, PS70–PS85)

Added with `docs/prd/round-14.md`. It covers CA / CS / CMA firms (client billing beside the practice desk), advocates and
law firms, architects and interior designers, consulting and IT / software agencies (including exports of services),
digital marketing and media agencies, and freelancers: billing models, disbursements, legal-services RCM, client money,
exports of services under the FEMA 2026 regulations, 194J / 194T, partners and Sec 40(b), and 44ADA / Sec 58.

- These are **planned** behaviours. The files named under "Where" are the ones Round 14 will create
  (`src/lib/prof-rules.ts`, `src/lib/prof/ps-*.ts`) or the existing files it appends to.
- Ids use the prefix `PS`. Points already asked elsewhere are not repeated: pure-agent reimbursements at 0 % (G7) and
  their gross P&L presentation (T25), retainer time of supply (T26), TDS rates and thresholds (T1, T2), Sec 393 labels
  (T16) and Form numbers (T17), the TDS receivable register (T12), exchange rate on export documents (X7), export
  realisation (X1) and Rule 96A (X2), intermediary indent agents (TR21). PS items add only what is new for professional
  firms; **PS4 and PS15 may change the answers to TR21 and X1**.
- **Two findings change existing behaviour** and are asked first: the intermediary place-of-supply rule was omitted by
  the Finance Act 2026 from 30 Mar 2026 (PS4), and the FEMA 2026 regulations as amended on 22 Sep 2026 set 9 months (12
  for INR) from 1 Oct 2026 where the app holds 15 (PS15).
- Some items are also questions for someone other than a CA: PS1, PS2, PS26–PS28 for an advocate / the Bar Council,
  PS29 for ICAI / ICSI / ICMAI, PS30 for an architect / the Council of Architecture, PS15–PS17 for the AD bank. Please say
  so where that applies.
- Sources: §2.6 of the PRD (P = primary text read, S = secondary, V = vendor).

### 14.1 GST on professional services

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PS1 ★ | **Legal services: reverse charge or exemption.** N 13/2017-CT(R) entry 2: legal services (incl. representational) by an individual advocate incl. a senior advocate, or a firm of advocates, to a business entity in the taxable territory → recipient pays (a litigant business entity is the recipient). N 12/2017-CT(R) entry 45 exempts supplies to another advocate / firm of advocates (not by a senior advocate), to a non-business person, and to a business entity whose aggregate turnover in the preceding FY is up to the registration threshold (wording since N 21/2019-CT(R)). (a) Is "client has a GSTIN, or says it is a business above the threshold" a fair default for RCM? (b) A senior advocate's fee to an advocate: forward charge by the senior advocate? (c) Was the Delhi HC challenge (J.K. Mittal) decided? | (a) Yes; the user can change it per document. (b) Forward charge. (c) Not tracked. | `prof-rules.ts` `LEGAL_SERVICES_RULES`; `prof/ps-legal.ts` | 2017-07-01; threshold wording 2019-10-01 | N 13/2017 corrigendum and CBIC press release 15 Jul 2017 (primary); gstgyaan, cleartax, caclubindia |
| PS2 | **Advocates who are not registered.** An advocate whose supplies are all under reverse charge or exempt need not register (Sec 23(2), N 5/2017-CT as recalled). The app lets such a profile issue a "Fee memo" without GST that stays out of GST returns. A law firm that also provides non-legal taxable services (tax filing, company formation): does that turnover count toward its own threshold, and does the exemption for legal services then stop? | As stated; non-legal lines are taxable at forward charge. | `prof/ps-legal.ts`, `ps-legal-paper.ts` | — | Recalled; caclubindia (mixed services) |
| PS3 ★ | **Pure-agent disbursements in returns.** New pure-agent lines (court fee, stamp duty, ROC / government fees, client-authorised costs paid at actual) will be left out of GSTR-1 Table 8, the HSN summary, 3.1(c), the e-Invoice item list (kept in `OthChrg`) and the e-Way item list, like the R8 pure-agent charges (QA8 T9). Invoices already issued reported such lines as **nil-rated**; the app will not rewrite them and lists them per period. (a) Is the exclusion right? (b) Should past periods be amended in GSTR-1A / the next return? (c) Travel, hotel, courier and photocopies incurred in our own name are treated as part of our taxable fee (markup 0 %) — right? | (a) Excluded. (b) Listed only. (c) Taxable. | `prof/ps-disb.ts`; `timesheets.ts` (`expenseLine`); `trade/pure-agent.ts` | — | Rule 33 (CBIC page, primary heading); gstzen, taxguru, busy.in |
| PS4 ★ | **Intermediary after the Finance Act 2026.** Sec 157 of the Finance Act 2026 omitted Sec 13(8)(b) IGST; the place of supply of intermediary services is now the location of the recipient (Sec 13(2)), so services to a foreign principal can be an export if Sec 2(6) is met. (a) Effective date: 30 Mar 2026 (assent; no separate commencement as read)? (b) Invoices raised before that date for services after it, and advances received before it — which rule? (c) Is there a saving for earlier periods? This also changes TR21 (indent agents). | Dated rule: supplier's location to 29 Mar 2026, Sec 13(2) from 30 Mar 2026; by invoice date. | `prof-rules.ts` `INTERMEDIARY_POS_RULES`; `trade/tr-indents.ts` | 2026-03-30 | Grant Thornton (read); taxguru, AMSS, ICAI CA Journal, winvesta |
| PS5 ★ | **Export of services — the five conditions (Sec 2(6) IGST).** The app checks: supplier in India; recipient outside India (no fixed establishment in India receiving the service); place of supply outside India; payment in convertible foreign exchange or in INR where RBI permits (Special Rupee Vostro Accounts — Circular 202/14/2023-GST); not merely establishments of a distinct person (Explanation 1 to Sec 8). It warns only. (a) Is a payment received through PayPal / Wise / Payoneer in INR into the Indian account "convertible foreign exchange"? (b) Payment by an Indian group company on behalf of the foreign recipient? | Platform receipts treated as foreign exchange when the platform's statement shows the foreign currency; payment by a third party in India fails (iv). | `prof/ps-export-check.ts` | — | Circular 202/14/2023-GST (primary); taxguru, fintaxblog |
| PS6 ★ | **Design services and immovable property.** Architects', interior designers' and surveyors' services directly in relation to a property: place of supply = location of the property (Sec 12(3) / 13(4) IGST), so designing a building in India for a foreign client is not an export, and the State of the property decides CGST + SGST or IGST. Is that right for concept / design work done in our office before any site exists? Interior design of a rented office for a tenant? | As stated. | `prof/ps-property-pos.ts`; `ps-export-check.ts` | — | Recalled (statute) |
| PS7 | **Exchange rate for services (Rule 34(2)).** The INR value of a foreign-currency service invoice uses the rate "as per generally accepted accounting principles on the date of time of supply". The app uses the currency master (RBI / FBIL reference) rate on the invoice date and lets the user type the bank's rate. Acceptable? (One ICAI-hosted copy of Rule 34 still shows the older RBI-reference wording.) | As stated. | `currency-rates.ts`; `prof/ps-fx-rates.ts` | 2017-07-27 | gstzen, taxguru, caclub (secondary, agree); ICAI copy (older) |
| PS8 | **Platform receipts.** A client pays USD 2,200; Payoneer / PayPal / Upwork keeps USD 55 and credits the rest. (a) The export value stays the invoice value; the fee is our expense — right? (b) Is the platform's fee an import of services on which a registered exporter pays IGST under reverse charge (Sec 5(3) IGST), or an OIDAR supply? (c) Does the short receipt affect the Sec 2(6)(iv) condition or the refund (Rule 89(4)) for that invoice? | (a) Yes. (b) Import RCM when the platform is non-resident and we are registered. (c) Refund on the amount realised. | `prof/ps-platforms.ts` | — | Developers' reading |
| PS9 | **Success fees and time of supply.** A consultant's (or arranger's) success fee becomes payable on an event (funding closed, recovery made). The app raises the invoice when the event is marked done. Is the time of supply the event / invoice date (Sec 13(2)), and is anything due on the retainer part earlier? | Invoice on the event date. | `prof/ps-models.ts` | — | Developers' reading |
| PS10 | **Deposits and advance fees.** Money a client deposits for court fees and expenses (client money) is not consideration until applied (Sec 2(31) proviso, recalled) — no GST on receipt. An "advance fee" is an advance for services, taxable on receipt (existing advances, 5.3). The app asks "for fees or for expenses?" on each deposit. Is that the right line? When client money is later transferred to fees, is GST due on the invoice date only? | As stated. | `prof/ps-client-money.ts`; `advance-tax.ts` | — | Recalled |
| PS11 | **Architects and interior designers — rates.** Architectural services (SAC 998321) and interior design services: 18 %. An interior designer who designs and also executes the fit-out under one contract — a works contract (immovable property, SAC 9954) at the works-contract rate, or two supplies? Materials bought on the client's behalf: part of our supply (with markup) unless bought in the client's name and paid as pure agent? | Design 18 %; execution a separate engagement with a typed rate; materials part of supply unless pure agent. | `prof/ps-stages.ts` | — | Recalled |
| PS12 ★ | **Media buying by agencies.** (a) Space / airtime bought in the agency's own name and billed to the client: the agency is a principal; is the whole bill taxed at the agency's rate (18 %), or may the media portion carry the media's own rate (print-media space 5 %)? (b) When the client contracts with the media / platform and the agency only pays on its behalf, is Rule 33 met? (c) Commission received from a publisher: a separate supply to the publisher? | (a) Principal, the line class chosen by the user (no automatic 5 %). (b) Pure agent only when marked. (c) Separate invoice. | `prof/ps-media.ts` | — | Recalled; vjmglobal (194C side) |
| PS13 | **Registration threshold for service providers.** ₹20 lakh (₹10 lakh special-category States) of aggregate turnover, which **includes exports** of services; inter-State service suppliers below it need not register (N 10/2017-IT). Which compulsory-registration cases in Sec 24 should the threshold card mention for freelancers (e.g. supplies through e-commerce operators, OIDAR)? | Exports counted; warnings at 80 % / 100 %; Sec 24 cases listed as "check". | `prof-rules.ts` `REGISTRATION_THRESHOLD_RULES`; `prof/ps-threshold.ts` | 2017-07-01 | gstzen, taxheal (N 10/2017-IT); taxtmi forum (exports) |
| PS14 | **Refund for exports of services under LUT (Rule 89(4)).** Turnover of zero-rated supply of services = payments received in the period for services exported, plus advances received earlier for services completed in the period, less advances for services not yet completed; refund = that turnover × net ITC ÷ adjusted total turnover. The statement lists invoice, value, FIRA / e-BRC no. and date, realised value. Is the definition current, and is the e-BRC or the FIRA the document the refund officer expects? | As stated; both numbers carried. | `prof/ps-refund-svc.ts`; `gst-cash-refunds.ts` | — | Rule 89(4) recalled; vjmglobal, winvesta |

| PS15 ★ | **Realisation period from 1 Oct 2026.** RBI's FEM (Export and Import of Goods and Services) Regulations 2026 (FEMA 23(R)/2026-RB, 13 Jan 2026, in force 1 Oct 2026), as amended by FEMA 23(R)(1)/2026-RB (22 Sep 2026): **9 months from the invoice date** for services (Reg 5(1)(a)), **12 months** when invoiced or settled in INR, extendable by the AD bank. (b) Do goods exports from 1 Oct 2026 also move to 9 months (Reg 5(1)(a), shipment date)? (c) Does the 12-month INR rule apply only to invoices raised in INR or also to FX invoices settled in INR? | (a) Yes, by invoice / shipment date. (b) Yes — the row applies to goods too. (c) Either. | PS16 | **Export Declaration Form for services.** From 1 Oct 2026 an EDF replaces SOFTEX: for software, one EDF for the month's invoices within 30 days of the month end, certified by the AD bank or STPI; services other than software may file by the date of receipt; invoices up to ₹10 lakh may be closed on the exporter's declaration (Reg 3(2), 4(2)). Is our reading right, and which services count as "software" (SaaS subscriptions, custom development, IT-enabled services)? | Register with due dates; the user picks software / services per client; number typed after filing. | `prof/ps-edf.ts`; `prof-rules.ts` `EDF_RULES` | 2026-10-01 | RBI notification 13277 (primary); taxheal, ispirt |

Foreign bank charges deducted abroad: a reduction the exporter declares (Reg 6, ≤ ₹10 lakh), not an unrealised amount? | As stated. | `prof/ps-brc.ts`; `trade/export-register.ts` | 2026-10-01 | vjmglobal, india-briefing, winvesta, RBI Reg 6 (primary) |

| PS61 | **EDF builder.** "Software" = any line with SAC 998313–998319 or 997331, else "services"; one draft EDF per month × kind × currency, due 30 days after the month end (services may instead file by the receipt date); a row may be closed on the exporter's declaration only when every invoice in it is up to ₹10 lakh (INR value at the invoice rate). Is one EDF per month and currency right, and is the ₹10 lakh test per invoice? | As stated; the user types the EDF no. after filing. | `prof/ps-edf.ts`; `prof-rules.ts` `EDF_RULES` | 2026-10-01 | FEMA 23(R)/2026-RB Reg 3(2), 4(2) |

The platform's fee is booked as an expense bill (SAC 997159), under import-of-services reverse charge (IGST 18 %) when the platform is non-resident and we are registered. Right? | As stated. | `prof/ps-brc.ts`, `prof/ps-platforms.ts` | — | Developers' reading |

| PS26 ★ | **Contingent and success fees.** BCI Rules Part VI Ch. II Sec. II Rule 20: an advocate shall not stipulate a fee contingent on the results of litigation or share in the proceeds. CA Act First Schedule Part I clause (10): no fee on a percentage of profits or contingent on findings / results, except as Regulation 192 permits (receivers / liquidators, IBC fees, court-fixed fees — as reported). The app lets a `legal` or `ca` profile choose a success-fee model only after a warning and an owner reason. (a) Is a warning (not a block) acceptable? (b) Do non-litigation advisory mandates of advocates (e.g. transaction work) fall under Rule 20? (c) Similar clauses for CSs and CMAs? | Warning + reason, audited. | `prof/ps-engagements.ts` | — | ipleaders, Kluwer arbitration blog; MCA First Schedule (via vaquill); taxclue, bcajonline |
| PS27 ★ | **Client money.** BCI Rule 25: accounts of client's money showing amounts received; Rule 26: whether received for fees or expenses; Rules 27–28 (as reported): not diverted, a copy of the account on request. CA Act Second Schedule (recalled): a client's money other than fees, or money to be spent, kept in a separate bank account and used for its purpose within a reasonable time. The app: a separate bank account flagged "client money", a ledger per client / matter that can never go negative, transfer to fees only against an issued invoice with a recorded authority, a three-way reconciliation and statements. (a) Anything missing (e.g. time limits for applying or refunding deposits)? (b) Interest earned on a client-money account — the client's or the firm's? | As stated; interest asked once (`interest_to`). | `prof/ps-client-money.ts`, `ps-cm-reco.ts` | — | Lok Sabha reply reproducing Rules 25–26 (primary); jurigram; CA Second Schedule recalled |
| PS28 | **Conflict checks.** BCI Rule 33: an advocate who has advised or acted for a party in a matter shall not act for the opposite party in it without the express consent of all concerned; switching sides in the same proceedings is misconduct; unrelated matters generally are not. The app searches names / PAN / GSTIN across clients, vendors and every matter's parties and records "clear / conflict / waived" with a consent note; it never blocks. Enough as a record? Should consent be in writing (the app stores a reference only)? | As stated. | `prof/ps-conflicts.ts` | — | ipleaders, livelaw, scconline, lawweb |
| PS29 | **UDIN.** ICAI requires a UDIN on certificates (from 1 Feb 2019), GST and tax audit reports (1 Apr 2019) and other attest work (1 Jul 2019); the assessee's PAN is mandatory for the "GST & Tax Audit" category (as reported, May 2025). The app keeps a register (UDIN, date, client, kind, member no.), checks an 18-character format, prints the UDIN on the billed line and lists UDINs not yet billed. (a) Is printing the UDIN on the fee note acceptable practice? (b) Format rule (year + membership no. + 10 characters) — current? (c) Do ICSI and ICMAI UDINs follow the same pattern? | As stated; format check marked verify. | `prof/ps-udin.ts`; `prof-rules.ts` `UDIN_FORMAT_RULES` | 2019-02-01 | ICAI post 15505, UDIN FAQs (Jul 2024); taxmann, moneylife |

The app ships only an editable example ("agree with your client") and recomputes stage bills when the project cost is revised. Any objection to recomputing earlier stages on a revision (catch-up on the next bill, credit note when lower)? | As stated. | `prof/ps-stages.ts`; `prof-rules.ts` `DESIGN_STAGE_EXAMPLE` | — | studiomatrx, solatatech (secondary, conflicting) |

Should the editor's amount follow the fee base? | Section and rate filled; the user may adjust. | `components/prof/Tds194jHint.tsx`; `prof/ps-tds-recv.ts` | 2025-04-01 | Recalled (Circular 23/2017) |

Right for the 2025 Act forms? | As stated. | `prof/ps-tds-recv.ts` `chaseList`, `chaseLetter` | 2026-04-01 | Rule 31 (recalled); `ita2025.ts` |
| PS78 | **Cash share for 44ADA / Sec 58.** Cash receipts ÷ all receipts of the FY, counting receipts that moved money (advance adjustments, write-offs and other no-cash rows left out), foreign-currency receipts at the invoice's rate. Is "receipts in cash" the right base (rather than cash ÷ gross receipts on accrual), and does an advance received in cash count in its own year? | As stated. | `prof/ps-presumptive.ts` | 2023-04-01 | taxguru, cleartax (secondary) |
| PS79 | **Billable compliance events.** GSTR-1 and IFF bill as "GSTR-1", GSTR-3B and CMP-08 as "GSTR-3B", GSTR-9 and GSTR-4 as "annual return"; a DSC is "renewed" in the month its validity starts; the monthly checklist bills once a month when any item is ticked. Any event a CA firm would bill differently (per GSTIN, per quarter)? | As stated; typed events for the rest. | `prof/ps-ca-events.ts` | — | Developers' reading |
| PS80 | **UDIN on the fee note.** The UDIN of a certificate billed prints in the line's description and in the document's `ps` snapshot; a revoked UDIN is not offered for billing. Should a revoked UDIN already billed trigger a credit note prompt? | No prompt. | `prof/ps-udin.ts`; `prof/ps-ca-bill.ts` | 2019-02-01 | ICAI UDIN FAQs (Jul 2024) |

### 14.9 Questions raised while building PS-B (disbursements, legal, client money, design, media)

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PS50 | **One "Client money held" ledger.** The books carry one liability ledger "Client money held"; the per-client (and per-matter) balances are kept in the client-money register (`client_money`, CMV/FY…) and printed in the client statements and the year-end list. Is one control ledger with a client-wise register acceptable for BCI Rule 25 / the CA Act, or should each client have its own ledger in the books? | One control ledger + client-wise register. | `prof/ps-cm-post.ts` | — | Developers' reading |
| PS51 | **Interest earned on a client-money account that belongs to the firm.** When the firm keeps the interest (`interest_to: firm`), the app credits "Interest received" and the money stays in the client account until moved out; the three-way reconciliation lists it as a difference to clear. Should the app instead require it to be moved to the office account at once? Is interest on clients' money the firm's to keep at all? | Credited to the firm, listed as a reconciling item. | `prof/ps-cm-post.ts`, `ps-cm-reco.ts` | — | Developers' reading |
| PS52 | **Authority to apply client money to fees.** Moving client money to fees needs an issued invoice and a recorded authority (written, e-mail or a clause in the engagement letter); without it only the owner may proceed, with a reason (audited). Is a standing clause in the engagement letter enough, or is a fresh authority needed for each transfer (BCI Rules 26–27; CA Act)? | Any of the three; owner override audited. | `prof/ps-cm-post.ts` `recordClientMoney` | — | BCI Rules 25–26 (Lok Sabha reply); CA Act (recalled) |
| PS53 | **Which lines are "legal services".** The app applies RCM / exemption only to lines with SAC 99821x (legal advisory and representation; an empty SAC on a fee memo counts as legal) and treats 99822x (accounting / audit) and 99823x (tax consultancy and preparation) billed by a law firm as forward-charge supplies. Right? Are arbitral tribunals' services (entry 1 of N 13/2017) to be handled the same way? | As stated. | `prof/ps-b-rules.ts` `LEGAL_SERVICES_RULES.sac`; `ps-legal.ts` | — | SAC scheme (recalled) |
| PS54 | **A senior advocate's supply to another advocate.** As reported, entry 45 does not exempt it; the app then applies reverse charge (entry 2) when the recipient advocate is treated as a business entity. Should it instead be forward charge by the senior advocate? | Reverse charge, marked verify. | `prof/ps-legal.ts` (`senior_to_advocate`) | — | Secondary sources (gstgyaan, cleartax) |
| PS55 | **Media bought as principal.** The re-bill shows the media cost and the agency fee as two taxable lines at the agency's rate (18 %), unless the user picks the media's own class (e.g. print space 5 %) for the cost line. Is splitting the bill this way acceptable, or must a principal's bill be one composite supply at one rate? | Two lines; the media class only when the user picks it. | `prof/ps-media.ts` `mediaLines` | — | Developers' reading |
| PS56 | **Writing off an unrecovered disbursement.** The cost was booked as an expense when paid (gross posting, T25), so a write-off only removes it from the unrecovered list and posts nothing. Right, or should a recoverable (asset) posting and a write-off journal be used? | No journal. | `prof/ps-disb.ts` `writeOffDisbursement` | — | Developers' reading |


### 14.10 Added by QA round 17 (`docs/prd/qa-round-17.md`)

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PS81 | **Book profit with interest above 12 %.** When the deed allows more than 12 % on capital, the excess is disallowed under Sec 40(b)(iv); the run now adds that excess back when working out book profit (Explanation 3: profit computed under Sec 28–44D) before the remuneration limit. Right? (See PS72 for crediting the deed rate.) | Excess added back (QA17 R2). | `prof/ps-partner-run.ts` `computeRun` | 1997-04-01 | Sec 40(b) Expl. 3 (recalled) |
| PS82 | **Pure-agent recoveries in the other papers.** Rule 33 recoveries are left out of the GST registration turnover, the Rule 89(4) zero-rated turnover (each receipt scaled by the fee share of its invoice) and adjusted total turnover, the 194J fee base and 44ADA gross receipts; the EDF value and the FEMA realisation register keep the whole invoice value (the recovery is also to be realised in foreign exchange). Right on each — in particular, do reimbursements count in 44ADA gross receipts? | As stated (QA17 R3). | `trade/pure-agent.ts` `recoveryValue`; `prof/ps-threshold.ts`, `ps-refund-svc.ts`, `ps-tds-recv.ts`, `ps-presumptive.ts`, `ps-edf.ts` | — | Rule 33 CGST Rules; Sec 2(6) CGST (aggregate turnover); developers' reading |
| PS83 ★ | **Realisation period 5 Jun – 30 Sep 2026.** FEMA 23(R)/(8)/2026-RB (First Amendment Regulations 2026, Reg 9), in force 5 Jun 2026, as reported cut the 15 months back to 9. The app now gives 9 months to exports invoiced / shipped from 5 Jun 2026 and keeps 15 for 13 Nov 2025 – 4 Jun 2026. Does the 9 months apply only to exports made from 5 Jun, and is anything outstanding on that day still on 15? | As stated (dated row, verify). | `trade/trade-rules.ts` `EXPORT_REALISATION_MONTHS_RULES` | 2026-06-05 | taxmann (5 Jun 2026 notification), thetaxcorp, winvesta |
| PS84 | **Partner-wise note.** The annual pack's partner-wise table shows opening, introduced, drawings, interest, remuneration (gross), share of profit, a column "TDS 194T and other" (TDS deducted, interest on drawings, any other entry) and closing, so each row foots. Is that presentation acceptable for the partners' capital note, or should TDS and interest on drawings be separate columns? | One balancing column (QA17 R7). | `ledger/notes-auto.ts` | — | Developers' reading |
| PS85 | **Pure-agent expense with ITC.** An expense marked "pure agent" is recovered at its total including GST even if the bill was marked ITC-eligible (the R3 timesheet flow; the disbursement row turns ITC off for "on the client's behalf"). Rule 33 suggests a pure agent takes no ITC on it: should the app refuse ITC on a pure-agent expense, or recover only the taxable value when ITC is taken? | Unchanged; noted by QA17. | `timesheets.ts` `expenseLine`; `prof/ps-disb.ts` | — | Rule 33 CGST Rules (recalled) |

`PS` ids are counted once here. The ★ items (PS1, PS3, PS4, PS5, PS6, PS12, PS15, PS18, PS20, PS22, PS23, PS26, PS27)

## 15. Travel, tour and event businesses (Round 15, ids TV1–TV48)

Added with `docs/prd/round-15.md` (§9: TV1–TV31), R15-B (TV32, TV33), R15-C (TV34–TV37), R15-D (TV38–TV40), QA round 18 (TV41–TV45, §15.1) and R16-C (TV46–TV48, §15.2). It covers tour operators (domestic, outbound,
inbound / DMCs), travel and ticketing agents (IATA / BSP or through a consolidator) and event / wedding / MICE planners,
plus the round's cross-cutting items (party netting, the 2026 manpower and tour-package TCS changes).

- Files named under "Where" with a package in brackets are planned by that package (R15-C / R15-D); the others exist
  since R15-A / R15-B. Every rule row in `src/lib/travel-rules.ts` is `verified: false` unless its value was read in a
  primary text in Round 15 (stricter than R14).
- Points already asked elsewhere are not repeated: pure-agent recoveries at 0 % (G7) and their gross presentation (T25),
  Rule 33 for professionals (PS3), hotel tariff bands (HT items), 194H (T1, T2), Sec 393 labels (T16).
- ★ = changes what a document prints or a return reports. Sources: §2.8 of the PRD (P = primary text read, S = secondary).

## 16. Platform round and the Oct 2026 – Apr 2027 statutory watch (Round 16, ids PF1–PF18)

Added with `docs/prd/round-16.md` (§9). PF1–PF11 are recommendations of the 57th GST Council (8 Oct 2026) that are
**not yet notified**: the app keeps them in a pending list (`src/lib/pending-changes.ts`, notes only, never rule rows;
Help → What's new → "Announced, not yet in force") until a notification is issued. PF12–PF13 are help notes, PF14–PF16
belong to R16-B / R16-C, PF17–PF18 to R16-D.

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PF1 ★ | **Inverted-duty refund and input services (57th Council ¶8.2–8.3).** From which notification / date does Net ITC for an inverted-duty refund include input services (ITC availed on or after 1 Nov 2026)? Capital goods over 60 months from 1 Apr 2027: per-month apportionment? | Pending note only; formula unchanged (inputs only) | `pending-changes.ts`; Manufacturing → Inverted duty (`mf/mf-inverted.ts`) | — (recommended 2026-11-01 / 2027-04-01) | PIB release 8 Oct 2026 (57th GST Council) ¶8.2–8.3 |
| PF2 | **Rule 89(4)(C) 1.5× cap removal:** effective date; does it also cover services? | Pending row; cap still applied | `pending-changes.ts`; `gst-cash-refunds.ts`, `prof/ps-refund-svc.ts` | — | PIB release 8 Oct 2026 (57th GST Council) ¶6.2 |
| PF3 ★ | **Late fee waiver** for turnover ≤ ₹5 crore when the return is filed by the end of the month in which it was due: from which period; GSTR-1 too, or only Sec 39(1) returns? | Pending note on the Compliance tab; late fee still computed | `pending-changes.ts`; `gst-penalties.ts`; GST → Compliance | — | PIB release 8 Oct 2026 (57th GST Council) ¶15 |

| PF6 | **Sec 13(3)(a) IGST omitted:** which of our job-work / repair exports move to Sec 13(2)? | Not modelled (watch) | `prof/ps-pos.ts` | — | PIB release 8 Oct 2026 (57th GST Council) ¶10 |

Is the set-off between two differently named ledgers of one party presented right? | Vendor's and client's own sub-ledgers; derived on read, no migration | `ledger/postings.ts` (`receiptVoucher`, netting rows only); `netting.ts` | — | — (design) |
| PF18 | **Real-estate no-ITC caution (R16-D3).** A builder (realty pack) on the 1 % / 5 % scheme (N 03/2019-CT(R)) is cautioned on every bill that claims ITC, also when the bill is not charged to a project (no caution while a project is on the old 8 % / 12 % option); saving past it is audited with the bill's note as the reason. Which inward supplies stay eligible (e.g. capital goods for a commercial let-out)? | Caution on every ITC claim; override audited | `bill-guard.ts` `realtyItcCaution` | 2019-04-01 | N 03/2019-CT(R) (recalled, CN18 / CN34) |

### 16.1 Added by R16-B (Round 14 engines wired)

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PF19 | **Per-appearance billing on a fee note.** With "Bill appearances" the entries of an appearance work type become one line "count × fee" (unit NOS, the firm's SAC, no hours, no dates on the line); the legal-services regime (RCM / exempt, PS1, PS2) is read as for any fee line, and the time still counts for realisation. Does a court fee note need each appearance's date (or the hearing list) printed, and is one line per work type and fee acceptable? | One line per work type × fee; dates stay in the app (the timesheet annexure lists hourly rows only). | `prof/ps-legal.ts` `appearanceLines`; `prof/ps-prebill.ts` | — | Developers' reading |
| PF20 | **Publisher's commission invoice.** The pre-bill's "Bill commission to the publisher" makes a separate draft invoice to the publisher (matched to a client of that name, else a new name) with one line "Media commission n % — publication" at the firm's SAC and GST rate (PS12 (c)). Which SAC (e.g. 998363 / 998365) and is it forward charge at 18 %? Should the expected TDS 194H by the publisher be shown on it? | Firm's SAC and rate from the time preferences; no TDS hint. | `prof/ps-media.ts` `commissionLine`; `prof/ps-prebill.ts` `createCommissionDraft` | — | PS12; developers' reading |
| PF21 | **Which WIP is foreign for the closing-rate memo.** The year-end memo treats a matter as foreign-currency when its active engagement bills in a foreign currency, and restates the net unbilled figure at the master's rate on the closing date; a work-type rate card in a foreign currency on an INR engagement is not converted. Open foreign invoices use the existing revaluation (balance on the closing date × the master's rate). Is the engagement currency the right test? | Engagement currency; memo only (PF14, PS37). | `components/prof/YearEndWipView.tsx`; `prof/ps-fx-rates.ts` `atClosing`; `fx-gain.ts` `revalueReceivables` | — | AS 11 (recalled) |

### 16.2 Added by QA round 19 (`docs/prd/qa-round-19.md`)

| Id | Question | App's assumption | Where | From | Source used |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PF22 | **Re-issue refund as a credit note (QA19 U7).** When a re-issued ticket is cheaper, the fare difference is credited on the original invoice's pure-agent fare line (Rule 33, 0 %) by value: the note's line carries exactly the difference (quantity = difference ÷ fare, e.g. 0.1495 of the ticket). It has no taxable value, so it reaches no GSTR-1 table. Is a credit note against the old invoice the right document for a refunded fare at actual (vs a refund voucher only), and may its quantity be a fraction of the ticket? | Credit note by value on the fare line; no GST; no GSTR-1 value. | `travel/tv-reissue.ts`; `documents.ts` `buildCreditNote` (`CreditLineRequest.amount`) | — | Developers' reading (TV35, PF16) |
| PF23 | **Netting in the Tally / CSV exports (QA19 U8).** A netting receipt to a hand-linked vendor is exported as a Journal: Dr the supplier's own ledger (Agst Ref the bill no.) / Cr the client's ledger (Agst Ref the invoice no.); the receipts CSV names the supplier's ledger as "Deposit To". Is a Journal (not a Contra / Receipt) what the auditor expects in Tally for a set-off between two ledgers of one party? | Journal, as for R9 exchanges (QA10 R5). | `tally-xml.ts`, `accounting-export.ts` `receipts`, `export-shared.ts` `exchangeCreditorLedger` | — | PF17; developers' reading |

`PF` ids are counted once here. The ★ items (PF1, PF3, PF5, PF7, PF17) change what a document prints or a return
reports once notified.

## 17. Security, facility-management and manpower-supply agencies (Round 17, ids MP1–MP48)

Added with `docs/prd/round-17.md` (§9: MP1–MP32) by R17-A; MP33–MP36 by R17-B; MP37–MP40 by R17-C; MP41–MP44 by R17-D; MP45–MP48 by QA round 20 (`docs/prd/qa-round-20.md`). It covers private security agencies (PSARA licensees),
housekeeping / facility-management contractors, contract and temporary staffing firms and labour contractors outside
construction (R11 CN-D2 keeps those).

- Files named under "Where" with a package in brackets are planned by that package (R17-B … R17-D); the others exist
  since R17-A. Every rule row in `src/lib/manpower-rules.ts` is `verified: false` unless its value was read in a primary
  text in Round 17 (its source then says "Read (R17)").
- No minimum-wage or VDA amounts are stored: the user types each revision (PRD §1.6). Labour-law outputs are hints.
- ★ = changes what a document prints or a return reports. Sources: §2.8 of the PRD (P = primary text read, S = secondary).

The hint counts distinct people on the month's musters across all sites (no day-wise data). Right proxy? Does a PSARA-licensed security agency also need the OSH contractor licence in each State it deploys in? | Hint only, when no OSH / contract-labour licence is on file | `mp-wage-check.ts` `labourHints` (`clraAll`), `CONTRACT_LABOUR_THRESHOLD_RULES` | 2025-11-21 | P OSH Code 2020 Sec 45(1) and Sec 27 (Gazette of India, Extraordinary, 29 Sep 2020, read in QA20); commencement S.O. 5321(E) not read (labour.gov.in 404) |
| MP46 ★ | Arrears note date and TDS (QA20 V6): a debit note / supplementary invoice for wage-revision arrears is dated the later of the day it is made and its original bill's date (a bill dated the month end or the 1st of the next month can be ahead of today); its expected client TDS is the threshold test on that day with the bill counted, else the bill's own `mp.tds` rate when the bill is still a draft. Right — or should the note follow the client's actual credit date? | As stated | `mp-draft-io.ts` `makeArrears`, `documents.ts` `createDebitNote` (`date`) | — | Sec 34 CGST Act (recalled); Sec 393(1) Table Sl. 6 (MP14) |
| MP47 | Bills of several sites (QA20 V3): each line carries its site (cost centre) and the service charge is one line per site, so the cost-centre P&L allocates the whole bill; the site P&L shares a bill's taxable value by its lines (credit notes by their own lines). Payroll cost stays on the employee's home cost centre in the books, while the site P&L apportions it by duties (relievers at other sites differ). Acceptable? | As stated | `mp-bill.ts` `billPlans`, `mp-profit.ts` `billedFor` | — | — (MP25, MP26) |
| MP48 | Muster corrections after billing / payroll (QA20 V7): a muster billed by a live bill, or of a month whose payroll run is final, cannot be unlocked — duty corrections go through a credit / debit note on the bill (or cancelling it) and a reopened run. The compliance pack apportions each run line by cumulative duties over the month's locked musters in site order, so the last site absorbs the paisa (QA20 V5). Right? | As stated | `mp-muster-save.ts` `setMusterLock`, `mp-compliance.ts` `siteShares` | — | — (MP26) |
`MP` ids are counted once here. The ★ items (MP1, MP2, MP4, MP6, MP7, MP10, MP12, MP14, MP16, MP29, MP34, MP41, MP46) change what a
document prints or a return reports.

## 18. Co-operative housing societies, apartment owners' associations and RWAs (Round 18, ids HS1–HS44)

Added with `docs/prd/round-18.md` (§9: HS1–HS44) by R18-A. It covers co-operative housing societies (State co-op
Acts), apartment owners' associations (apartment-ownership Acts), resident welfare associations, commercial premises
societies and maintenance agencies billing owners.

- Files named under "Where" with a package in brackets are planned by that package (R18-C, R18-D); the others exist
  since R18-A or R18-B. Every rule row in `src/lib/society-rules.ts` is `verified: false` unless its value was read in a primary
  text in Round 18 (its source then says "Read (R18)").
- **State co-operative / apartment rules are data rows keyed by State code and date** (`STATE_SOCIETY_RULES`), never
  code branches. A bill not yet in force (the Karnataka Bill 2026) is a row with `in_force: false` that applies only
  after the user confirms it. Bye-law and State checks are hints, never blocks.
- ★ = changes what a document prints or a return reports. Sources: §2.8 of the PRD (P = primary text read, S = secondary).

## Answers log

Each CA answer is added here as one row — date, id, answer (OK / Change / Depends, with the value and source) and the
PR that applied it as a new dated rule row.

| Date | Id | Answer | PR |
|---|---|---|---|

---

## Counting note

Each id is counted once, in the area where it is listed. Points raised in several documents (for example the
settlement discount in Round 2, the collections doc and the QA report) were merged into one id. Gift vouchers (G47)
and branch transfers (G48) come from the retail-chains pack but are GST questions, so they sit in area 1.
T24–T26 (depreciation, pure-agent presentation, retainer time of supply) were raised on the TDS and service screens.

## Sources this pack was built from

- Feature documents: `docs/features/*.md`, especially the "Verify with your CA" / "Open questions" / "Statutory values"
  sections of `gst-reports`, `gst-setoff`, `composition-amendments`, `advances`, `purchases`, `purchase-returns`,
  `job-work`, `einvoice`, `docs-compliance-r3`, `tds-payables`, `income-tax-act-2025`, `payroll`, `ledger`,
  `books-output-r2`, `collections-r2`, `sales-r2`, `service-billing-r3`, `business-extras`, `retail-chains-r4`,
  `trade-trust-r4`, `production-r4`, `restaurant`, `marketplace-r5`, `transport-works-r5`,
  `institutions-property-r5`.
- Open-question lists: `docs/prd/round-2.md` §8, `round-3.md` §8, `round-4.md` §7, `round-5.md` §7.
- Rule tables, read directly from the code: `src/lib/tax-rules.ts`, `tds-rules.ts`, `gst-penalties.ts`,
  `gst-guards.ts`, `payroll-rules.ts`, `ita2025.ts`, `iff.ts`, `eway-validity.ts`, `transport-works-rules.ts`,
  `r5c-rules.ts`, `trade/trade-rules.ts`, `marketplace/returns-desk.ts`.

When a CA answer changes a value, the developer appends a new dated row to the table named in "Where" (never edits
an old one), adds a test, and records the answer here with the date and the CA's name.
